Tauheed, Bab 8 «اسماء»: hub plan

18 lessons from 8 Fasls. Every lesson opens and closes: tap a title. Inside, The steps is open; the rest is folded. Source refs are hidden; the Refs button shows them. Comment by selecting text, or with a section's button.

new taught here first · ↺ B3 S04 partly taught there; the new part is taught here · ↺ B1 S03 taught there; one-line reminder here · core repeat · ask kept in full, you decide · ask for you · Claude's judgement an import kept on Claude's framing

Gate D: for Ali to approve

Each item has my pick. Per-lesson Asks stay in each entry; Ali answers them on Reading Mode.

  1. 18 lessons from 8 Fasls (the diagnosis table): U8.1 two (S01, S02), U8.3 two (S03, S04), U8.5 three (S05 to S07), U8.6 one (S08), U8.7 six (S09 to S14), U8.8 one (S15), U8.9 one (S16), U8.11 two (S17, S18). Fold test (a fold where recalls empty a lesson): none is emptied, because Bab 8's own subject, the names as names, is mostly new (198 points taught here first). Fallback 14 (diagnosis): U8.3 one lesson, U8.5 two, U8.7 four. Recommended: 18.
  2. Three structure Asks the readers kept open: S04 closes with U8.8 B (the words said of God) as its section; S13 (U8.7 D2, asking the Pardoner) may become U8.11's lesson C instead; S18 carries U8.11 C (the sinner before the Generous) as its step 5. Recommended: keep as drafted.
  3. S06 repeats much of B4 S11 (Huwa, Ahad, Samad, Imam Husayn's letter, al-Baqir's close): 2 SAME, 7 PART, 2 core repeats, only 3 points new; its Ask 1 asks whether to fold it. Recommended: keep S06, since the reading of the names as names (the hidden alif and lam, the ten meanings of al-Samad) is new, and recall B4 S11 for the rest.
  4. Verdicts the readers changed after reading both passages (each an Ask in its entry): U8.1.13 SAME to PART and U8.1.39 PART to NONE (S02); U8.3.33 PART to SAME (S03); U8.8.4 SAME to PART (S04); U8.5.28 PART to SAME (S05); U8.7.84 NONE to PART (S11); U8.7.79 PART to SAME (S14). Recommended: accept.
  5. The flagged clash on 53:5 (U8.7.102 against B6 S06) is not one: S11's reader found B6 S06 leaves both readings open. Recommended: rule them with the earlier Bab's Asks.

The 18 lessons

اللہ کے نام اس کی نشانیاں ہیں: اس نے خود اپنے نام رکھے اور ہر مخلوق اس کا تکوینی نام ہے؛ نام مشترک ہو سکتا ہے، معنی نہیں؛ «اللہ»، «هو»، «الصمد» اور «بسم اللہ» اسی کی پہچان کراتے ہیں؛ نور، اول و آخر، خالق، غفار اور جمال و جلال کے نام اس کے کمالات کی جہتیں ہیں؛ حروف اور اسمِ اعظم ان ناموں کے راز ہیں؛ اور بندہ انہی ناموں سے اسے پکارتا ہے، اور وہ مجیب ہے۔

1. What a name is; creatures as His names S01 to S02

S01 «اسم یعنی نشانی: خدا نے خود اپنے لیے نام رکھے تاکہ مخلوق اسے پہچانے اور انہی ناموں سے پکارے، اور یہ نام اسے گھیرتے نہیں نہ اس پر بدلتے ہیں»

ledger H01 · 15 new · 8 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why begin the knowledge of God with His names? The subject is vast: after many sessions on the single word Allah the discussion has only begun, the first thousand of the "ten thousand doors" of knowledge is the thousand names, each name of 59:23 is a door of its own (His might is other than His grandeur, His guarding other than His granting safety), and the Imam explained the verses of tawhid by expounding the names. (EZ P001:5-6, P002:4-18 without naming the teacher, P004:14; AH P005:1-3, P006:1-8 with the 59:23 block; EZ P010:12-14 one line, stages of tawhid; U8.1.1, U8.1.2, U8.1.3, U8.1.4). [U8.1.1 PART: recall B4 S06 "the word of tawhid is a summary door whose detail is thousands upon thousands of doors (AH)" then new: EZ's witness that the subject of the names had only begun, P001:5-6 | U8.1.2 NEW (MM's own text MMB_01_S04 ¶125 to replace EZ's report if gathered, Ask 3) | U8.1.3 PART: recall B4 S11 (and B6 S01) "al-Baqir's teaching on Huwa and al-Samad" then new: the Imam explains the tawhid verses by expounding the names, P004:14 only | U8.1.4 PART: recall B1 S12 "59:22 as God's own self-introduction (JS)" then new: 59:22-24 as the Quran's teaching of the names, each name a separate door (AH P006:1-8), the stages of tawhid the Prophet conveys (EZ P010:12-14)]
  2. Why did God name Himself? He is known only through Himself: every man knows Him by fitra and is bewildered in grasping Him, so He described Himself with names and made signs for His Essence; He chose them so that He comes out from behind the veil of the Unseen and creatures can address Him, call on Him and stand in relation with Him; al-Rida: "then He described Himself with names and called creation, when He created them, to call on Him by them". (MM P011:5 with the Sahifa line P011:3 trimmed to the perception clause; AH P028:1-4; JS P061:9-10 with al-Rida's text (key), P066:27-28, P064:4, P064:8-9; 7:180 once, JS P012 block; U8.1.5, U8.1.15, U8.1.16). [U8.1.5 PART: recall B1 S11 and B3 S14 "God is described by verbal names and known by the signs that point to Him" then new: He named Himself and made signs for His Essence because He is known only through Himself, MM P011:5, AH P028:1-4 | U8.1.15 NEW (first half: chosen so servants call on Him, JS P064; second half in step 5) | U8.1.16 NEW]
  3. What is a name? A sign: ism comes from wasm, the brand burnt on a herd animal that tells whose it is; in Arabic ism, sifa, dalil, aya all mean sign, as a man's own name, his family name and his title are each a sign. (EZ P017:5-27, P082:15-27, P046:2-12, P043:6-14 one example set; U8.1.28, U8.1.11; U8.1.29 as one teacher line). [U8.1.28 NEW | U8.1.11 NEW | U8.1.29 NEW, Teacher Copy only]
  4. Is a name the same as an attribute? In the lexicon a name is wider (Zayd names a man and tells nothing of him); in revelation every name of God carries a meaning, "an attribute of the described", and both words mean sign; our names may be mere addresses, His names always carry meaning. Huwa and Allah, as pure pointers to the Essence, are the exceptions in one line (pointer to S05 and S15). (JS P023:2-4, P012:4-5, P013:8, P014:6-8, P015:5-6, P019:14-26, P023:4, P022:10-13; MM P018:7-8 with «صفة لموصوف» (key); EZ P025:3-7; EZ P084:11-27, P085:9-20 last line; U8.1.6, U8.1.31; fine layer U8.1.24, U8.1.25, U8.1.26). [U8.1.6 PART: recall B7 S01 "the names Living, Knowing, Powerful are the very content of His attributes" then new: the explicit identity of name and attribute in revelation, MM P018:7-8, JS P023:2-4, P022:9, EZ P025:3-7 | U8.1.31 NEW | U8.1.24, U8.1.25 NEW, Teacher Copy only | U8.1.26 PART, Teacher Copy only: recall B7 S04 "JS's division of the revealed names into positive and negative" then new: the threefold division into names of the Essence, of the acts and of transcendence, JS P079:1-7]
  5. Do the names take hold of Him? No: every name has a meaning and every attribute a described, and they point to His perfection and being without encompassing Him; He is pointed to by His attributes, reached by His names and inferred from His creatures, and if the names did not call to Him, worship would go to names and not to Him. (MM P056:3-4, al-Rida trimmed to «واعلم أنه لا تكون صفة ... ويستدل عليه بخلقه» with the "worship would go astray" clause; U8.1.15). [U8.1.15 NEW]
  6. Do the names change over Him as they do over a man or a date? No: man is dust, then flesh, then blood, then bone, and the date unripe, ripening, ripe, each stage with a new name; He is on one state and the names do not alternate over Him; and the many meanings of the names do not make many in Him. (MM P054:4 trimmed to the alternation clause, 57:3 frame to S10; JS P055:5-17; U8.1.14). [U8.1.14 NEW]
  7. Are all His names alike? All are holy: "but You, Your names are holy" (Kumayl), He has no bad name (17:110) and no small name; yet some are higher, a great name and a greatest name, which opens the Greatest Name (pointer to S16). (AH P045:1-8, P029:5-8; EZ P044:14 one line; JS P008:3-4 17:110 block as the one line; EZ P044:9-10 bridge line; U8.1.10, U8.1.9). [U8.1.10 NEW | U8.1.9 PART: recall B3 S04 (and B3 S03) "signs differ in degree, and the Infallibles are the greatest sign" and B4 S06 "Amir al-Mu'minin called «لا إله إلا هو» the Greatest Name" then new: the names themselves have degrees, a great and a greatest name, EZ P044:9-14; the degrees of the Greatest Name (EZ P044:16-19, AH P029:1-4) go to S16]
  8. Close: He gave the names so that He is known and called by them; how creatures themselves are His names and words is the next lesson.
Asks for Ali 19
  1. Gather the AH/MM records named in POINTS.tsv but in no gather (MM MMB_01_S04 ¶125; AH HLB_01_S06 ¶999-1001; AH MV-S08 ¶24), so AH/MM carry steps 1 and 3? Recommended yes (diag U8.1 Q1).

  3. U8.1.2: EZ's report of the "ten thousand doors" names his teacher; keep it as an unnamed report, or replace it by MM's own text MMB_01_S04 ¶125 once gathered? Recommended MM's text (diag Q3, ledger ASK). The teacher's Arabic list (P002:6-10) in Urdu only (diag Q9).
  4. U8.1.3: Handout keeps only EZ P004:14; the Jabir background and «يبقر العلم» (P004:2-13, late collections only) to the Teacher Copy? Recommended yes (diag Q4, ledger ASK).
  5. U8.1.6 doubt: kept PART with a one-line B7 S01 recall although Bab 1 to 6 found nothing; or teach it as NONE with no recall? Recommended PART as written (Repeats).
  6. RR8-006 SAME: AH's du'a sits under the recall of B1 S05 and B3 S16; teach again? Recommended no (Repeats).
  7. Step 1 and 2 texts: EZ's inline 59:23 (P010:12) duplicates AH's block, and EZ P010:1-11 (shahada, purity, slaughter) is another point; keep only P010:12-14 as one line? JS P008:6-8 (20:8, 59:24) to the Teacher Copy? Recommended yes to both (diag section 4, B2; section 5).
  8. U8.1.19: MM's P072 block (division of the Quran, 24:35, the Shajara Tayyiba hadith, 13:14) has no tie to the names beyond P072:1; keep one Teacher Copy line, 24:35 to S09, the hadith to U8.10 or Imamat? Recommended yes (diag Q7, ledger ASK).
  9. U8.1.24, U8.1.25: non-masum Arabic (al-Mizan P077, Majlisi P078:6-7) in Urdu only, no author named; may the Teacher Copy say «ایک تفسیر میں»? Recommended Urdu only, «ایک تفسیر میں» allowed (diag Q9, ledger ASK).
  10. U8.1.21: leave EZ P074 (the altered names; ends by deferring to another course) out of the hub, HELD for Imamat; and P073 (sayr fi al-Haqq) to S17? Recommended yes (diag Q10, ledger ASK).
  11. U8.1.23, U8.1.27: ikhtiyar from khayr (P076) and hujja with «إما ظاهرا مشهودا» (P081) answer no question about the names; teacher note in S01 only? Recommended yes (diag Q11, ledger ASK). If kept, P081 takes Jami's wording «إما ظاهرا مشهورا أو خائفا مغمورا» (Kumayl report), not «غائبا».
  12. U8.1.25: JS P078:9-19 contradicts itself in the recording; keep the refiner's reading ("every attribute may be called a name, not every name an attribute") in the Teacher Copy and check the audio? Recommended yes (diag Q8, ledger ASK).
  13. The Imams as the highest signs (EZ P042, P044:16-19) in S01: one Teacher Copy pointer line to U8.10 and S16, nothing in the Handout? Recommended yes (diag Q18).
  14. MM P011:3: the Sahifa line is not in the Sahifa as cited (Jami: Imam al-Sajjad, Tuhaf al-'uqul 283, Bihar 75:142); remove MM's Arabic frame and the stray «8», name the source from Jami; and the du'a name «مشمول» (probably «مشلول»)? Recommended yes (diag Q19c, Q20).
  15. MM P054:4: restore al-Sadiq's hadith from al-Kafi 1:167 / al-Tawhid instead of Majma' al-Bahrayn, trimmed to the alternation clause, 57:3 frame to S10? Recommended yes (diag Q20, section 4).
  16. MM P018:8: print «صفة لموصوف» as in Jami, and name the Imam as al-Kafi leaves him (unnamed)? Recommended yes (UNVERIFIED-TEXTS row 3). The Na'thal narration MM calls "explicit" (P018:9) is never given: drop the sentence or gather the text? Recommended drop in the Handout, flag in the Teacher Copy.

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. ﴿هُوَ اللَّهُ الَّذِي لَا إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا هُوَ الْمَلِكُ الْقُدُّوسُ السَّلَامُ الْمُؤْمِنُ الْمُهَيْمِنُ﴾ (59:23, AH P006 to P007): flagged reprint, B4 S07; first and full use in this Bab, later lessons recall it.
  2. ﴿وَلِلَّهِ الْأَسْمَاءُ الْحُسْنَىٰ فَادْعُوهُ بِهَا﴾ (7:180, JS P012): once in the Bab in full, here; the H2 and H3 copies are cut as blocks.
  3. «ثُمَّ وَصَفَ نَفْسَهُ تَبَارَكَ وَتَعَالَى بِأَسْمَاءٍ دَعَا الْخَلْقَ إِذْ خَلَقَهُمْ وَتَعَبَّدَهُمْ وَابْتَلَاهُمْ إِلَى أَنْ يَدْعُوهُ بِهَا» (al-Rida, JS P061:9): FOUND, al-Kafi, 'Uyun, al-Tawhid, Bihar 4:177-178.

  5. «وَاعْلَمْ أَنَّهُ لَا تَكُونُ صِفَةٌ لِغَيْرِ مَوْصُوفٍ وَلَا اسْمٌ لِغَيْرِ مَعْنًى ... وَيُسْتَدَلُّ عَلَيْهِ بِخَلْقِهِ» (al-Rida to 'Imran al-Sabi, MM P056:3-4, trimmed): FOUND, 'Uyun, al-Tawhid, Bihar 10:315-316.
  6. «لَيْسَ شَيْءٌ إِلَّا يَبِيدُ وَيَتَغَيَّرُ ... لَا يَخْتَلِفُ عَلَيْهِ الصِّفَاتُ وَالْأَسْمَاءُ كَمَا تَخْتَلِفُ عَلَى غَيْرِهِ» (al-Sadiq, MM P054:4, trimmed): the printed wording is only in Majma' al-Bahrayn 3:202; the hadith itself is in al-Kafi 1:167 (Ibn Abi Ya'fur) and al-Tawhid (Bihar 4:182-183): restore from there.

Next lesson picks up

if a name is a sign, are spoken words His only names, or is every creature also His name and His word? «اگر اسم نشانی ہے تو کیا زبان پر آنے والے الفاظ ہی اس کے نام ہیں، یا ہر مخلوق بھی اس کا نام اور اس کا کلمہ ہے؟» (S02)

S02 خدا کے نام صرف زبان کے الفاظ نہیں، اس کی ہر مخلوق اس کا تکوینی نام اور اس کا کلمہ ہے جو اس کی پوشیدگی کو آشکار کرتی ہے؛ یہ نام اور کلمات درجوں میں ایک دوسرے سے بڑھ کر ہیں اور ختم نہیں ہوتے، مگر وہ خود ان میں سے کسی سے متعین نہیں ہوتا، اور عقل اس کی ذات کو نہیں، صرف ان سچے عنوانات کو پاتی ہے جو اس نے خود اپنے لیے بتائے

ledger H02 · 12 new · 8 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Are spoken words His only names? No: a sign is either set by agreement (a word, a horn, a whistle) or real (a thing); so God has verbal names, the names of the Jawshan "up to the thousand and one", and cosmic names: not the word "sun" but the sun's existence is His name; every thing He made is His cosmic name, and of names there are three kinds (cosmic, legislated, verbal), each cosmic name doing a work (the apple for the heart, the quince for the stomach); as a man's cloak and turban on the table and his voice are his existential names, so the beings are God's (EZ U8.1-1 P031:1-10, P035:1-9, P032:14-17, P038:3-10; AH P034:1-4; JS P030:3-7 one sentence; EZ U8.1-3 P086:11-25, P087:1-3, P087:9-12, P087:21-28; U8.1.8, U8.1.7, U8.1.32, U8.1.33; U8.1.30 one TC line). [U8.1.8 PART: recall B3 S14 "God is described by verbal names and by cosmic signs" and B1 S03 "the thousand names of the Jawshan" then new: the two kinds framed as verbal and creational names, the count of 1001, in EZ P031:1-4 and AH P034:1-4. U8.1.7 PART: recall B3 S03 "every thing is His sign; man the greatest creational sign" then new: the beings called names of God, in EZ P031:5-7, P038:3-10 and JS P030:3-7. U8.1.32 NEW; U8.1.33 NEW (cloak on the table, Q16); U8.1.30 NEW, TC]
  2. Is every thing a sign of God? What He created is; what man makes is the sign of its maker: the sugar bowl is the silversmith's sign, the cloak the weaver's and tailor's; the tea plant is God's, the tea brewed in the cup is the brewer's, and no one blames God when salt goes in for sugar (EZ U8.1-4 P090:1-11, P091:1-24, one example set; U8.1.34). ↺ B3 S03 · B7 S03Already taught: B3 S03 "what God made, mountains, sun and moon, are His signs" (and B7 S03, a book shows its author)New here: a man-made thing names its human maker and not God, in P090:1-11 and P091:13-17. The "name" set between Muslims and polytheists (P090:13-17, U8.1.37) one TC line, flagged: Jami has the text, the name stopped the arrows, and EZ's stone reading is not in it (Q15)
  3. How are creatures His names without His being one of them? They are His determinations and manifestations while He is never determined by them and stays on His non-determination from eternity to eternity; we do not predicate the cosmic names of Him as we say "God is Knowing", yet inferring Him from His signs is His own self-description: «هو الذي يريكم آياته» (AH U8.1-1 P028:5-13; JS P030:2-2, P021:4-14; MM P027:1-3 with 67:21 and 40:13; U8.1.7 new part, U8.1.12 MM half). [U8.1.7 PART new part: AH's determinations, taught in full, AH's sentence as the reconciliation of JS and MM (Q17), no "two views" label. U8.1.12 PART, MM half: MM's doctrine that inference by the signs is His self-description, P027:3. AH's mithl and mathal (RR8-056) and the opposites as mirrors (RR8-061, TC) are SAME and sit under the B1 S05 and B1 S06 recalls]
  4. Why is a creature called His word (kalima)? «ألم تر كيف ضرب الله مثلا كلمة طيبة» opens the question; kalima is what makes the hidden plain, as the white bone shows through the red flesh under the knife («کلم العظم»); a sound thrown out by the tongue is a lafz, and a lafz that carries meaning is a kalima: sound, lafz, kalima (EZ U8.1-2 P049:8-24 one telling, P052:9-16 one telling, P053:10; the desert-Arab method in one TC line, P075:1-14; U8.1.38, U8.1.39; U8.1.22 TC). [U8.1.38 NEW; U8.1.39 NEW (changed from PART, see Repeats); U8.1.22 NEW, TC]
  5. So what is the world? His words: every thing that shows the hidden God is His word, as the voice from the next room shows the speaker; and His words do not run out: not a single hair of your body will be fully read by the scholars till the Day, nor the atom, «قل لو كان البحر مدادا لكلمات ربي لنفد البحر قبل أن تنفد كلمات ربي»; Christ is "a word from Him" (3:45) (EZ P049:61-72, P050:3-28 one example; AH U8.1-1 P029:7-24 with 18:109; EZ U8.1-4 P098:1-8 with the 3:45 block once; U8.1.13, U8.1.40 first half; U8.1.41 TC). [U8.1.13 PART (changed from SAME, see Repeats): recall B7 S17 "His words are His creative words, 31:27 (EZ, Teacher Copy)" then new: AH's hair and atom with 18:109, in P029:10-24. EZ's 31:27 (P049:71) sits under the recall. U8.1.40 PART (weak): recall B7 S17 "His act is called His word (the du'a «يا من فعله قول وقوله أمر»)" then new: Christ "a word from Him", P098:1-8. AH's world as a book (RR8-037) is SAME and sits under the B1 S05 recall; the creational word as the chain of proofs (RR8-004, TC) is SAME under the B1 S10 recall]
  6. Are His signs and words of one rank? No: the forest and snow peak against the smoke and litter in the tunnel, the sunset over the sea against the pebbles of the Karaj desert; the sun is great to us and small against the galaxy; His words are small and great like an author's lines, and His words have a highest, «وكلمة الله هي العليا»: what is highest is His word, not the word "Allah" the signboards write large (EZ U8.1-4 P092:1-25 poet unnamed, U8.1-1 P033:6-16, U8.1-4 P099:7-13, P101:11-18 with 9:40; AH P029:7-9 one line, every human a great cosmic name; U8.1.35, U8.1.40 second half, U8.1.42; the Imams as the highest words one TC line, pointer to U8.10, Q18). [U8.1.35 PART: recall B3 S04 "the signs differ in degree; the greater sign shows His power more" then new: the Damavand and desert-pebbles image, the gradation read as greater names, in P092:9-25. U8.1.40 second half PART (same recall as step 5): words small and great, P099:7-13. U8.1.42 NEW. The names of the Proof (RR8-016, TC) is PART: base recalled from B1 S11, the new list one TC line here. The perfect man as the mirror of all perfections (RR8-063, TC) is SAME under the B1 S11 recall]
  7. What do we owe His signs? To see them as His: believing in no god but He is tawhid of the Essence, and knowing every attribute and sign as His is tawhid of attributes; so at every door and wall, remember God (3:191); and the Lawh: «عظم يا محمد أسمائي واشكر نعمائي ولا تجحد آلائي» (EZ U8.1-2 P048:16-22 with 3:191; EZ U8.1-4 P093:3-13, the Lawh restored as one block, Q20; U8.1.12 EZ half, U8.1.36). [U8.1.12 PART, EZ half: recall B3 S03 "doors and walls are signs telling of the holy Essence (41:53)" then new: EZ naming this seeing tawhid of attributes as practice, P048:16-20. U8.1.36 NEW. AH's tongue of legislation and tongue of creation (RR8-036) is SAME and sits under the B1 S05 recall]
  8. Then what do we know of Him? Not the Essence nor the attributes that are the Essence: only the true titles, thing, existent, god, knowing, living, powerful, which He Himself gave and told us to call Him by; and denying them denies His own word: al-Rida to Sulayman, «فكيف أخبر الله عز وجل أنه واحد حي سميع بصير عليم خبير وهو لا يعلم ذلك... وهذا رد ما قال وتكذيبه» (JS U8.1-4 P102:1-18 in Urdu, the annotator unnamed, Q9; JS P103:28-31 with one line of setting, Q14; U8.1.43, U8.1.44). [U8.1.43 NEW; U8.1.44 NEW] This returns lesson B to lesson A's question "why did He name Himself" and closes the Fasl.

Next lesson picks up

اللہ کے نام، جیسے عالم، قادر اور سمیع، ہم پر بھی بولے جاتے ہیں؛ تو کیا اس سے ہم اس جیسے ہو جاتے ہیں؟ (God's names, such as Knowing, Powerful and Hearing, are said of us too; does that make us like Him?) (S03)

2. One name, a different meaning S03 to S04

S03 «اللہ کے کئی نام بندوں پر بھی بولے جاتے ہیں، مگر ان میں صرف نام مشترک ہے، معنی نہیں: وہ اپنی ذات سے عالم، سمیع اور واحد ہے اور ہم اس کے دیے سے، اس لیے اس میں اور ہم میں کوئی حقیقی چیز مشترک نہیں»

ledger H03 · 8 new · 6 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. God's names are said of us too: "God is knowing" and "Zayd is knowing". Does that make us like Him? The deniers put exactly this to the Imams: "you say nothing is like Him, so how did you come to share His beautiful names? That shows you are like Him." (JS H1 P093:10-14 bridge; MM H1 P054:2; JS H2 P163:6, 11 block 3, the objection opens the lesson; H2 P163:1-3 name list as one Urdu line, Ask 5; U8.3.28. Teacher Copy: JS poses the question as rational, not lexical, H2 P123:1-14, U8.3.16.) [U8.3.28 NEW | U8.3.16 NEW, fine layer]
  2. What did the Imam answer? God made servants bear some of His names with different meanings, and addressed them in what they understand, so it is a proof against them: a man is called dog, donkey, ox, sugar, without being any of them; likening is in meanings, the names are one; so too "lion" for the lion and for brave Zayd. (MM H1 P082:3, P053:10, P053:25 merged with JS H1 P099:4 as one block, «سکّرة» fixed, Ask 8; JS H1 P099:5-9 or H3 P169:9-23, one lion line only; MM H1 P053:26 landing; U8.3.6, U8.3.30. Teacher Copy: a student's claim that names signify by convention, with JS's answer, H3 P175:2-15, U8.3.34, Ask 12.) [U8.3.6 NEW | U8.3.30 NEW | U8.3.34 NEW, fine layer]
  3. But "one" is said of God and of man: is the oneness alike? No: "you have said the impossible; likening is in meanings, the names are one and point to the named"; man is one body of parts, God is one in meaning: «الله واحد أحدي المعنى والإنسان واحد ثنوي المعنى». (MM H1 P066:1-2 blocks 5 and 6, cut at «وهي دالة على المسمى», Ask 7; MM H1 P053:11 text 7, the Prophet named, Ask 10; JS H1 P069:3-11; candidate AH* HLB_01_S12 ¶1884-1887, Ask 3; U8.3.7.) ↺ B3 S09Already taught: B3 S09 "the Latif section of al-Rida's answer to Fath ibn Yazid: Latif for His subtle creation and His knowing of it"New here: the "God is one and man is one, one in name, not in meaning; neither tashbih nor ta'til" section, in MM H1 P066:1-2 and P053:11, JS H1 P069:3-11
  4. Are all names shared alike? No: the sifat are His and their names run on creatures, like Hearing and Seeing; the nu'ut fit Him alone: "light with no darkness, life with no death, knowledge with no ignorance". (AH H1 P083:6-24, block 8 extended to the nu'ut clause; JS H1 P092:9-39 as one Hisham ibn Salim block, «أعلم الناس» fixed, Ask 9; JS H1 P092:26-35 inference, garbled Urdu at P092:32-35 rewritten; EZ H1 P098:16-18 one line, al-Samad is His alone, taught in S06; candidate MM* MMB_16_S01 ¶8-9, MMB_07_S07 ¶286, Ask 3; U8.3.8.) new
  5. Then what does "Knowing" or "Hearing" mean for Him? He is called Knowing because He is ignorant of nothing, and creatures are called knowing for a knowledge that came to them; so Creator and creature share the name and the meaning differs. The zindiq asked, "can one hear except by an ear?"; the answer: Hearing without an ear, Seeing without an eye, Subtle without the work of a hand; "and so all the names". (JS H1 P099:11, P099:14-15 with H2 P139:19 as one block, blocks 11 and 12; H2 P139:28 block 13; H1 P099:10 block 10 trimmed or dropped, Ask 6; JS H3 P171:1-28, P172:4-5 block 14 as the question; H3 P173:3-12 and H1 P102:2-11 the answer, «صنعتهم» and «السحماء» fixed, Ask 9; EZ H1 P097:6-10 with 42:11 one line; U8.3.10, U8.3.31, U8.3.11; the black atom image U8.3.32 under its recall; Teacher Copy: al-Rida's ten names as the rule for all names, H3 P166:1-8, P167:13-15, U8.3.29; qa'im, not as standing on legs, H3 P174:1-9, U8.3.33 under its recall.) [U8.3.10 PART: recall B7 S03 "the signs show that He is not ignorant (also S07, He knows secret and open)" then new: that is why He is called Knowing, and the name is shared while the meaning differs, in JS H1 P099:11, P099:14-15, H2 P139:19, 28 | U8.3.31 PART: recall B2 S09 "the same al-Rida and zindiq munazara: He is seeing, not like the seeing of His creatures, and the zindiq became Muslim" then new: the zindiq's objection that hearing needs an ear and seeing an eye, in JS H3 P171:1-28, P172:4-5 | U8.3.11 NEW | U8.3.32 SAME: recall only | U8.3.33 SAME: recall only (verdict changed, see Repeats) | U8.3.29 NEW, fine layer]
  6. And where does our knowing come from? He is Knowing, Living, Hearing by His Essence, we by Him; what is in His Essence is beginningless, unlimited and by itself, our knowledge is begun, limited and from another; so in that by which knowing occurs nothing is common between Him and us: "no common factor", nothing at all joins Creator and creature. (EZ H1 P097:19-23, P097:25-38 with 2:255 one line; JS H1 P034:2-8; MM's «لا جامع بين الخالق والمخلوق» in MM's own wording first (MMB_03_S04 ¶129, if fetched, Ask 3), JS's report of it, H1 P035:2-4, P038:2-3, fine layer; U8.3.9, U8.3.3, U8.3.4. The limit on shared titles, RR8-045, under its recall.) [U8.3.9 PART: recall B7 S02 "His perfections are His own, ours His shadow and gift: O knower, you know through Me" then new: the same applied to the names said of both, God by His Essence and we by God, in EZ H1 P097:19-38 and JS's Tawhid bab 29 question | U8.3.3 PART: recall B3 S03 "between Him and creatures there is no sinkhiyya" (also B5 S02; B7 S05, no creaturely mark in Him) then new: "no common factor" in that by which knowing occurs, His beginningless and by itself, ours begun and from another, in JS H1 P034:2-8 | U8.3.4 PART: same recall, then new: MM's "no jami' at all" as the ground for the names, in JS H1 P035:3-4, fine layer]
  7. Landing: the name joins us, the meaning does not: «فقد جمعنا الاسم ولم يجمعنا المعنى». (MM H1 P053:25-26; JS H2 P139:28.) [NEW as landing]

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. al-Rida: «إن الله تبارك وتعالى ألزم العباد أسماء من أسمائه على اختلاف المعاني... وقد يقال للرجل كلب وحمار وثور وسكّرة وعلقمة وأسد» (al-Kafi; Mir'at al-'Uqul 2:56; Bihar 4:177). Jami FOUND, one merged block; «شكرة» to «سكّرة», «تزييع» to «تضييع ما ضيعوا»; tail «فقد جمعنا الاسم ولم يجمعنا المعنى» found (al-Shafi 294).
  2. The deniers, in the same hadith: «أخبرونا إذ زعمتم أنه لا مثل لله ولا شبه له كيف شاركتموه في أسمائه الحسنى» (Mir'at 2:56; Sharh al-Kafi 4:39). Jami FOUND; the block runs on to «في أسمائه الحسنى».
  3. Fath ibn Yazid and al-Rida: «فالله واحد والإنسان واحد أليس قد تشابهت الوحدانية» / «يا فتح أحلت ثبتك الله إنما التشبيه في المعاني فأما في الأسماء فهي واحدة وهي دالة على المسمى» (al-Kafi, Mir'at 2:51; 'Uyun, al-Tawhid). Jami FOUND-OTHER-WORDING: «أليس» for spoken «فليس», «فأما» for «و أما».
  4. The Prophet to a Jew: «الله واحد أحدي المعنى والإنسان واحد ثنوي المعنى» (al-'Udad al-qawiyya; Ghayat al-maram 142). Jami only in later collections; name the Prophet صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ.
  5. al-Sadiq: «فالصفات له وأسماؤها جارية على المخلوقين مثل السميع والبصير... والنعوت نعوت الذات لا يليق إلا بالله» (al-Tawhid; Bihar 4:68), with Hisham ibn Salim's «هو نور لا ظلمة فيه وحياة لا موت فيه وعلم لا جهل فيه» (Bihar 4:70). Jami FOUND; «وأنا أعلم الناس بالتوحيد» for «عالم الناس».
  6. al-Rida: «وإنما سمي الله عالما لأنه لا يجهل شيئا فقد جمع الخالق والمخلوق اسم العالم واختلف المعنى» (Mir'at 2:57) with «كما أنا رأينا علماء الخلق إنما سموا بالعلم لعلم حادث» (Bihar 4:177) and «فقد جمعنا الاسم واختلف المعنى وهكذا جميع الأسماء» (Mir'at 2:60). Jami FOUND; spoken «فقط» for «فقد».
  7. al-Rida and the zindiq: «أ يكون السميع إلا بالأذن» and «إنه سميع لا بأذن... بصير لا ببصر» ('Uyun, al-Tawhid; Bihar 4:175-176). Jami FOUND-OTHER-WORDING: «صنعتهم» for «صنعته», «السحماء» for «السمراء».
Next lesson picks up

«اگر اس کا علم ہمارے علم جیسا نہیں، تو کیا ہم اس کے بارے میں کچھ بھی نہیں جانتے، اور کیا اسے «شیء» کہا جا سکتا ہے؟» (If His knowing is not like ours, do we know nothing of Him, and may He be called a "thing"?) (S04)

S04 «اللہ شے ہے مگر اشیا کی مانند نہیں: یہ اثبات اسے تعطیل اور تشبیہ دونوں حدوں سے نکالتا ہے، اور اس پر بولا جانے والا ہر لفظ اسی قید کے ساتھ سمجھا جاتا ہے»

ledger H04 · 23 new · 4 part-taught before · 3 taught before · 2 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. If His Knowing is not the knowing we know, do we know nothing of Him (ta'til)? No: the Imams named a third way between negation and likening, affirmation without likening; "I did not limit Him, I affirmed Him, for there is no station between affirming and negating". (JS U8.3-3 P176:6-9, U8.3-1 P042:15-17; JS U8.3-2 P147:1-3 text 20 inline, al-Rida named after the Jami check; JS U8.3-1 P045:7-8 text 19; U8.3.35). [U8.3.35 NEW (the ta'til objection, opening the lesson); text 19 reprinted, flagged B3 S04; recall S03 (Lesson A) in one line for «واختلف المعنى» (U8.3-3 P176:1-5), which S03 prints in full]
  2. May He be called "thing"? Yes: the Imam said it takes Him out of the two limits, the limit of ta'til and the limit of tashbih; the Ihtijaj telling says "the limit of ibtal", the same aim. (JS U8.3-3 P177:1-10 block 17, P177:4-8 the ibtal variant one line, U8.3-1 P104:11-19 under it; MM U8.3-1 P003:3, "thing" and "He" only, one line; candidate AH* HLB_01_S04 ¶619 not gathered, Ask 6; U8.3.12, U8.3.36). [U8.3.12 core repeat kept in full (see Repeats) | U8.3.36 PART: recall B2 S09 "the two limits, ta'til and tashbih" and B3 S10 "the 'yes, a thing' hadith", then new: the Ihtijaj wording «حد الإبطال», in JS U8.3-3 P177:4-8, P177:11-12]
  3. Did God call Himself a thing? Yes: al-Rida's companion answered from 6:19 «قُلْ أَيُّ شَيْءٍ أَكْبَرُ شَهَادَةً قُلِ اللَّهُ», "a thing unlike things, since denying Him thinghood is voiding Him", and the Imam said "you spoke true and hit the mark". (JS U8.3-3 P178:8-26 block 18, P179:17-20 one line; U8.3.37). [U8.3.37 PART: recall B3 S10 "a thing unlike things; calling Him a thing keeps Him from negation" (also B5 S03), then new: the dialogue of al-Rida and Muhammad ibn 'Isa on 6:19, in JS U8.3-3 P178:11-26, P179:17-20, P180:23-26; 6:19 reprinted, flagged B1 S10]
  4. What does "unlike things" add? A thing in the reality of thinghood, yet neither body nor form; so too "existent, not from non-being", "Hearing, without an ear". (JS U8.3-1 P115:3-20 text 16 after the Jami check; MM U8.3-1 P001:17 the qualifiers, one line; JS U8.3-3 P181:3-6; candidate AH* HLB_01_S07 ¶1056-1059 not gathered, Ask 6; U8.3.13). [U8.3.13 core repeat kept in full (see Repeats) | RR8-031, RR8-057 SAME: recalled under this step]
  5. Then why so many negations? To shut out ta'til, not to deny Him; those who refused even "thing" and "existent" mistook the concept for what it is true of; what His Essence is stays beyond reach. (JS U8.3-3 P181:7-22, the Ramadan story under the recall; JS U8.3-1 P111:1-8, P008:7-17 short, book named, author not; U8.3.38, U8.3.40). [U8.3.38 SAME: recall only, "Recall B3 S01: ..." | U8.3.40 NEW, in JS U8.3-1 P111:1-8 (the long Majlisi report U8.3-3 P182 to P186 Teacher Copy only, Ask 4)]
  6. How then are the words said of Him read? With the rule just learned, nothing is like Him: istiwa' is mastery, not sitting, and He established Himself on the Throne "by the Name by which He named Himself"; He has no like (mithl) but His is the highest mathal; «لم يزل الله عليما» is right but not «لم يزل يعلم», since the agent form carries no occurring object; and some words, like worship, are never said of Him. (JS U8.8-1 P018:1-5, P020:2-5 with 42:11 inline; MM U8.8-1 P015:5 one clause of the Muhaj du'a; AH U8.8-1 P016:6-16 and JS P017:30-33 under the recall; JS U8.8-1 P018:7-14, P019:14-35 with 16:60; EZ U8.8-1 P023:6-14, JS P021:1-22; JS U8.8-1 P028:1-10, Ibn Arabi's commentators unnamed; U8.8.4, U8.8.5, U8.8.6, U8.8.11; teacher U8.8.13, .17, .18). [U8.8.4 PART (changed from SAME, see Repeats): recall B5 S11 "istiwa' is mastery and management, not sitting; AH: from the station of rahmaniyya", then new: MM's clause «وبالاسم الذي سمى به نفسه واستوى به على العرش» | U8.8.5 SAME: recall only | U8.8.6 NEW | U8.8.11 NEW | RR8-071 SAME: recalled beside worship | U8.8.13, .17 NEW, U8.8.18 SAME, all teacher, cut asked]
  7. (Teacher Copy) Is the shared word equivocal or univocal? The scholars differ on the concept (MM: verbal homonymy; JS: one concept "repelling non-being", univocal only on the negative side and equivocal on the existential side; AH and EZ: used in the general meaning), but all agree nothing real is shared. (MM U8.3-1 P001:16-17, P003:4, MMB_06_S10 ¶424 for "thing" said metaphorically; AH U8.3-1 P004:7-16; EZ U8.3-1 P029:17-19; JS U8.3-1 P023:4, P025:1 to P027:9 with the book example, P034:6-8, NAR_0112 ¶11-13; JS's survey AQD_0122 ¶14-18; U8.3.1, .2, .5, .17, .21, .24, .27; the survey of other authors .18, .19, .20, .22, .23, .25, .26, .39, .41 in Urdu, authors unnamed, Ask 3). [all NEW, Teacher Copy; the "four paths" contradiction label and numbered sides go (Ask 2)]
  8. What does this ask of us? To take on His character through His names, generous, merciful, covering faults, without likening: in Him the attribute is necessary, unlimited and His own, in us contingent and given. (JS U8.3-2 P120:5-13, one telling, «تخلقوا بأخلاق الله» given as "it is said", not as hadith; JS U8.3-3 P187 Majlisi's sixth reading under it if Ali keeps a reading of «على صورته»; EZ U8.3-2 P122:8-12, covering faults with «ستار العيوب»; U8.3.15, U8.3.42). [U8.3.15 PART: recall B1 S16 "acting on ethics shapes one by the character of God" and B7 S05 "man is not told to mirror all His attributes, he gets at most a likeness", then new: takhalluq through the names without likening, in JS U8.3-2 P120:5-13 and EZ P122:8-12 | U8.3.42 NEW (Ask 15) | RR8-070 SAME: recalled here]

Who carries it

JS carries the lesson (about 400 of 480 spans of the Fasl; steps 1, 3, 5 and 8 are JS with EZ in step 8). MM gives one line in step 2 ("thing" and "He"), one in step 4 (the qualifiers), his side in step 7 (TC) and the Muhaj clause in step 6. AH is new only in step 7 (U8.3-1 P004:7-16); his istiwa' (U8.8-1 P016) sits under the B5 S11 recall, and all four AH re-read records are SAME, so recalled. AH and MM are absent as carriers from steps 1, 3, 5 and 8. The atlas names uncollected AH*/MM* lines for steps 2 and 4 (HLB_01_S04 ¶619, HLB_01_S07 ¶1056-1059) and for step 6 (MMB_03_S06 ¶249): Ask 6.

AH re-read adds 14
  • Imports (thin lesson; from U11.28, shirk in knowing and describing God; 2 kept, 11 dropped; every one also an Ask, Asks 21 to 33):
    • kept U11.28/3 (MM, MMB_07_S06 ¶272: al-Husayn in Tuhaf, "beware these renegades who liken God to themselves ... rather He is Allah, nothing is like Him and He is the Hearing, the Seeing ... no namesake resembles Him"), Teacher Copy of step 4, not a ground. Claude's judgement the Imam joins the negation of likeness to the affirmation of Hearing and Seeing, and says no one sharing His name resembles Him; that is the "thing unlike things" of step 4 in another Masum's words, so it supports the step, not only adds MM's voice. Not in Babs 1 to 6 (checked vowel-free).
    • kept U11.28/12 (MM, MMB_06_S11 ¶447: whoever deviates in His names without knowledge associates without knowing, hence 12:106), Teacher Copy of step 4, not a ground. Claude's judgement carrying the creaturely meaning into a name said of Him is the deviation in the names that this line calls unknowing shirk, so the qualifier "unlike things" is what guards the believer when he uses His names. Sound but secondhand; the gather's hint names al-Tawhid 324 h1 as the source, unchecked: if it is the tail of al-Rida's hadith that S03 opens with (U8.3.28), it sits better beside S03's text.
    • Dropped U11.28/1 (al-Sadiq and al-Rida, likening is shirk; MM, AH, JS): already taught, al-Rida's «من شبه الله بخلقه فهو مشرك» is printed in full in B5 S01 (and inline in B3 S10), so it would be a recall, not a new voice. AH's copy (HLB_01_S07 ¶1060-1065) follows his ¶1056-1059, the candidate for step 4 (Ask 6).
    • Dropped U11.28/2 (al-Rida: the ghulat forged the tashbih and jabr reports): no sound framing; it is about forged reports and the ghulat (U11.32), not affirming without likening.
    • Dropped U11.28/4 (worship by imagination, by the name without the meaning): no sound framing; its subject is name and named (U8.2, M2), where the diagnosis also sends U8.3's own «من عبد الله بالتوهم» lines; JS's line is in B5 S03 TC.
    • Dropped U11.28/5 (Hisham's hadith, "Allah" from ilah): no sound framing; it belongs to U8.2 and is already in this Bab at U8.5-1 (S05).
    • Dropped U11.28/6 (knowing God by the imagination of hearts is shirk): already printed in full in B5 S03 (and B4 S01).
    • Dropped U11.28/7 (knowing God by a veil, image or likeness is shirk): already printed in full in B2 S11 (and B5 S03).
    • Dropped U11.28/8 (in, from or on a thing is shirk): no sound framing; its argument is place (B5 S11 has the same line inline), not naming.
    • Dropped U11.28/9 (Nahj 91, "the 'adilun lied"): already printed in full in B5 S08.
    • Dropped U11.28/10 (end of al-Rida's sermon, those who make equals lied): no sound framing; it only adds a voice to the warning.
    • Dropped U11.28/11 (MM: describing Him other than He described Himself is ilhad and shirk): no sound framing here; its claim is tawqif (U8.4, M2), where the diagnosis sends MM's 37:159-160 lines, and MM's side of step 7 is carried by his own U8.3 texts.
    • Dropped U11.28/16 (al-Rida: tashbih and jabr are kufr and shirk): same teaching as U11.28/1, taught in B5 S01; jabr is Adl.

Texts and Jami results 7

  2. «للناس في التوحيد ثلاثة مذاهب: نفي وتشبيه وإثبات بغير تشبيه ... والسبيل في الطريقة الثالثة إثبات بلا تشبيه», al-Rida to Muhammad ibn 'Isa, al-Tawhid (Jami #19: name al-Rida, restore the articles). It is the tail of the same hadith as text 4 (U8.3-3 P178:27-29 omitted): Ask 13.
  3. «يجوز أن يقال الله: إنه شيء؟ قال: نعم، يخرجه من الحدين: حد التعطيل وحد التشبيه», al-Jawad («أبو جعفر الثاني»), al-Kafi 1; Bihar 3:262 (Jami #34: spoken «حد الإبطال», «عن الحدين»). Reprinted, flagged B3 S10, B5 S03.
  4. «ما تقول إذا قيل لك أخبرني عن الله عز وجل أشيء هو أم لا ... فأقول إنه شيء لا كالأشياء إذ في نفي الشيئية عنه إبطاله ونفيه، قال لي صدقت وأصبت», al-Rida, al-Tawhid; Bihar 3:262-263 (Jami #44: inline «نفي شيئية» drops «ال»); with 6:19, reprinted, flagged B1 S10.
  5. «هو شيء بخلاف الأشياء، ارجع بقولي إلى إثبات معنى، وأنه شيء بحقيقة الشيئية غير أنه لا جسم ولا صورة», al-Sadiq to the zindiq, al-Kafi (Jami #33: the inline «ارجع بقولي شيء» adds «شيء»; print al-Kafi's wording).

Next lesson picks up

the name «اللہ» itself, what it names and what it gathers (S05, U8.5 A; S05 sets its Urdu question).

3. Allah, Huwa, Ahad, Samad; takbir, tasbih, tahmid S05 to S07

S05 «اللہ» وہ معبود ہے جس کی حقیقت میں عقلیں حیران ہیں اور جس کے سامنے حیران دل جھکتا ہے؛ یہ نام سب ناموں کا جامع ہے اور اس کے سوا کسی کا نام نہیں

ledger H05 · 7 new · 7 part-taught before · 3 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why does the call of the Quran rest on one name? Because the One it names is known to all by fitra and the prophets came only to remind of Him (MM P001:3-6 with 30:30 and 7:172 inline and the Nahj block P001:5, cut to the reminding clause; AH P004:1-11). [frame of the U8.5.1 core repeat: one bridge line "Recall B2 S04: the prophets remind of the One known by fitra"; the Nahj block is reprinted, flagged B1 S07; the Nahj clause «ليستأدوهم ميثاق فطرته» (P002:21) and «كل مولود» (P002:22) are U8.5.11, homed in S07]
  2. What does Allah mean? The worshipped One in whom creation is bewildered (تألّہ، وَلَہ) and to whom it turns (MM P001:6, P003:2 hadith of Amir al-Mu'minin, P002:20; JS P010:18-23 al-Baqir's hadith with the Arabs' usage; AH P004:8-26; EZ P014:1-12 one line). (U8.5.1) [CORE REPEAT kept in full, B2 S04: core repeat · ask below]
  3. Is this bewilderment a lack of knowing? No: it is what knowing Him requires, and even inference from His signs ends in it (MM P017:1-6, AH P018:1-4, AH P006:27-31 with the dua «يا من لا يعلم ما هو», EZ P013:22-24: U8.5.2; MM P019:1-2, AH P021:1-6, AH P020:1-4 only if simplified: U8.5.3). [SAME (U8.5.2): recall B2 S01 "every newborn knows Him by fitra and is bewildered in perceiving Him; that is why He is ilah" | PART (U8.5.3): recall B1 S05 "thinking on His Essence only adds bewilderment" then new: MM's frame that inference by the signs in the divine sciences ends in walah, and that is why He is called Allah and Huwa (MM P019:1-2); AH's "secret of divinity": He is seen only by His own eye, being without end (AH P021:1-6)]
  4. What does ilah add to bewilderment? A ma'luh: whoever is bewildered before Him bows with inborn humility, so ilah is the One worthy of worship (MM P022:2-9, Hisham hadith cut to «والإله يقتضي مألوها»; AH P023:4-15; JS P025:8-10 in Urdu). (U8.5.4; U8.5.5 as fine layer) [PART (U8.5.4): recall B2 S07 "ilah as the worshipped One requires worshippers (AH)" then new: the Hisham hadith «يقتضي مألوها» and MM's reading of it (P022:9), AH's humility within bewilderment (P023:4-15), JS's "Allah and al-Ilah: the One worthy of worship" (P025:4-10) | PART (U8.5.5, TC): recall B2 S04 "aliha as worship and bewilderment" then new: Saduq's aliha as fleeing for refuge, the dropped hamza (JS P027:1-28), AH's lah and layh, hidden and high (AH P023:1-3, P023:23-25, P033:3-27)]
  5. Where does a person meet this Allah? In hardship, when every hope and means is cut (MM P028:1-2, Askari hadith on the basmala; MM P028:3-7 the boat hadith; MM P028:10; JS P029:10-14, P030:1-2 one line). (U8.5.6) ↺ B2 S10recall only, "Recall B2 S10: Allah is He to whom every creature turns in hardship when hope in all else is cut; the boat hadith"
  6. Is Allah one name among others? No: it gathers all the names, so "Ya Allah" calls them all; and no one else is named by it, as Amir al-Mu'minin read «هل تعلم له سميا» (JS P041:4-15, AH P056:1-3, EZ P014:14-15, EZ,JS P060:5-10: U8.5.9; JS P103:4-11, EZ P102:1-6 one line, EZ P014:14-15: U8.5.22; JS P138:1-25 with 19:65: U8.5.31). The 'alam question, EZ's name or attribute question and JS's aside go to one teacher note (U8.5.8, .29, .30), with al-Lat and al-'Uzza (U8.5.58). [PART (U8.5.9): recall B4 S06 "the word Allah is the condensed whole of tawhid, pointing to the Essence that gathers all perfections" then new: Allah as the sum of the names, the station of "Ya Allah" (AH P056:1-3), every other name points to one attribute while Allah holds them all (JS P041:4-15) | PART (U8.5.22): recall B7 S05 step 5 "samadiyya and grandeur stay His alone; man gets at most a likeness" then new: which names are His alone as a naming question, JS "Allah and al-Rahman are never given to a creature" (P103:4-11), EZ "all shared but al-Samad" (P102:1-6) and "Allah said of no one else" (P014:14-15) | NEW (U8.5.31)]
  7. What then does «لا إله إلا الله» deny? Every ilah in whom minds are bewildered, except God (MM U8.5-3 P134:3). (U8.5.28) [SAME (verdict changed from PART, see Repeats): recall only, "Recall B2 S01: «لا» is like «ليس» and denies every being in whom minds and understanding are bewildered, but Allah"]
  8. Close: why has Allah no translation, and why do the Quran, the reports and the duas open with it? Khuda, Khudavand, Yazdan do not render it, the other religions' God is not its meaning, and «اللهم» is «يا الله» (EZ U8.5-4 P157:1-9, P158:4-15, one or two lines; EZ U8.5-1 P070:1-11). (U8.5.38, U8.5.37; U8.5.39 TC) new

Texts and Jami results

«اللَّهُ مَعْنَاهُ الْمَعْبُودُ الَّذِي يَأْلَهُ فِيهِ الْخَلْقُ وَيُؤْلَهُ إِلَيْهِ» (Amir al-Mu'minin, al-Tawhid; Jami found: Nawadir al-akhbar 1:74, Nur al-thaqalayn 5:708); «اللَّهُ مَعْنَاهُ الْمَعْبُودُ الَّذِي أَلِهَ الْخَلْقُ عَنْ دَرْكِ مَائِيَّتِهِ وَالْإِحَاطَةِ بِكَيْفِيَّتِهِ» (al-Baqir, Bihar 3:222; Jami found; Nawadir and Kanz read «ماهيته»); «مُشْتَقٌّ مِنْ إِلَهٍ، وَالْإِلَهُ يَقْتَضِي مَأْلُوهاً» (al-Sadiq to Hisham, al-Kafi 1:166; Jami found; some copies «أله»); «اللَّهُ هُوَ الَّذِي يُتَأَلَّهُ إِلَيْهِ عِنْدَ الْحَوَائِجِ وَالشَّدَائِدِ» (al-'Askari, Ma'ani al-akhbar, Bihar 89:240-241; the printed block is MM's own Arabic, verified=no: use Jami's wording); «يَا ابْنَ رَسُولِ اللَّهِ دُلَّنِي عَلَى اللَّهِ مَا هُوَ... هَلْ رَكِبْتَ سَفِينَةً قَطُّ» (al-Sadiq, Bihar 3:41; Jami found); «وَأَمَّا قَوْلُهُ هَلْ تَعْلَمُ لَهُ سَمِيّاً فَإِنَّ تَأْوِيلَهُ هَلْ تَعْلَمُ أَحَداً اسْمُهُ اللَّهُ غَيْرَ اللَّهِ» (Amir al-Mu'minin, al-Tawhid; Jami found: Kanz al-Daqa'iq 8:253-254) with 19:65; «وَاصْطَفَى سُبْحَانَهُ مِنْ وَلَدِهِ أَنْبِيَاءَ» (Nahj sermon 1; Jami found).

کیا کوئی ایسا نام بھی ہے جو اس کی طرف اشارہ کرے مگر اس کا وصف نہ کرے؟ (Is there a name that points to Him without describing Him? Huwa) (S06)

S06 سورۂ توحید کے نام اللہ کو دکھائے بغیر پہچنواتے ہیں: «هو» اس ذات کی طرف اشارہ ہے جو حواس سے غائب ہے، «الصمد» وہ ہے جس میں کوئی خلا اور کوئی کمی نہیں، اس کے سب روایتی معنی اسی ایک جڑ سے نکلتے ہیں اور خود قرآن اس کی تفسیر کرتا ہے، اور «احد» کی یکتائی گنتی کی یکتائی نہیں

ledger · 3 new · 7 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 2 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Is there a name that points to Him without describing Him? Huwa: «الله» names the Essence as the One in whom creation is bewildered, «هو» names Him as absent from the senses and perceptions; al-Baqir: the ha confirms the fixed, the waw points to the One absent from sight and touch; a name that only points, like a person's proper name, and so brings all His perfections with it (MM H1 P031:3, P003:1 hadith; AH H1 P034:1-4, P036:2-6; JS H1 P039:3-17, P037:17-21; U8.5.7). [Core repeat kept in full: B2 S16 (AH, Huwa as the hidden station), B4 S11 (JS, Huwa as absolute unseen, 2:3) and B6 S01 (MM, al-Baqir's ha and waw printed in full) teach it; the closest new wording is MM P031:3 (Allah and Huwa as two aspects of one Essence beyond ta'til and tashbih) and JS P039 (a name that points without describing). AH's round ha (P034:5-10, P036:7-14) goes to U8.8 (S15). Ask 2]
  2. How does the hidden divinity show in the name al-Samad itself? Its alif and lam are written but not spoken or heard: His divinity is hidden by its subtlety, while His lordship shows in creation, as a man looking at himself does not see his own soul (AH H1 P008:2-3; MM H5 P173:1-3 cut to the alif and lam; U8.5.50). [NEW: B4 S11 left the Baqir letters for U4.13 (M2), which has no written points. The sad, mim and dal to U8.8 (S15) or Teacher Copy, Ask 7]
  3. What does al-Samad mean? The one with no hollow: solid, needing nothing, the one all turn to; and AH: God is samad, full and not empty, so that our thought could find a way into Him (JS H2 P101:5-12 with the verified block H5 P176:13; EZ H2 P102:7-26 without the physics; AH* ML-S03 ¶28 one line; U8.5.21). [Core repeat kept in full: B4 S11 teaches «الذي ليس بمجوف» (MM P010:8-9) and JS's gloss (needs none, all need Him); new only AH's inference that thought cannot enter Him, in AH* ML-S03 ¶28. Ask 3]
  4. Why so many meanings? About ten narrated meanings, with no clash, because all say one thing, the negation of every lack: the one whose mastery has reached its end, the obeyed master with no commander above Him, who neither eats nor sleeps, the ever-lasting; Imam Sajjad: no partner, guarding all things does not tire Him, nothing is hidden from Him (JS H5 P171:6-9, P172:4-8; JS H3 P124:8-16 inline; EZ H2 P101:13-28; Imam Sajjad H2 P099:8 with P099:9-26; U8.5.49, U8.5.26; U8.5.51 one Teacher Copy line). ↺ B4 S11Already taught: B4 S11 "al-Samad is not hollow, needs none, all turn to Him"New here: the range of narrated meanings and their one root (JS P172:4-8), and the obeyed master who neither eats nor sleeps (JS P124:8-16, EZ P101:13-28). U8.5.51 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B7 S11 "when He wills a thing He says to it Be (36:82)" then new: this given as a narrated meaning of al-Samad, with pairs and kinds (JS U8.5-5 P175:6-7), speaker Ask 9
  5. Who explains al-Samad? The Quran itself: Imam Husayn wrote that God explained al-Samad by «لم يلد ولم يولد ولم يكن له كفوا أحد»; and «الله» is said again in «الله الصمد» because each sentence of the sura by itself makes God known (JS H2 P096, P097:14-19; EZ H2 P100:7-9; MM* MMB_07_S02 ¶92, AH* MV-S09 ¶75 as corroboration; JS H5 P178:7-17; U8.5.20, U8.5.54; U8.5.53 Teacher Copy). [SAME U8.5.20: recall only, B4 S11 teaches the letter in full from MM; the letter is printed again in the Teacher Copy, flagged. PART U8.5.54: recall B4 S11 "EZ asks, among his tadabbur questions, why Allah is repeated (unanswered there, TC only)" then new: JS's answer, in P178:9-17. U8.5.53 SAME: recall B7 S03]
  6. And Ahad? One beyond number, «الأحد بلا تأويل عدد»; the technical difference of ahad and wahid is not what matters about God (JS H5 P179:2-8, P180:8-10; EZ H3 P133:10-24 one example at most; U8.5.55; U8.5.27, .56, .57 Teacher Copy). ↺ B4 S11 · B3 S03Already taught: B4 S11 "ahad is oneness of meaning, not of number (al-Jawad; al-Baqir «أحدي المعنى»)" and B3 S03 (the Nahj sermon printed in full)New here: JS's remark in P179:7-8. JS's Camel telling (P179:7-8, P180:8-10) and RR8-059 sit under the B1 S12 recall
  7. Close: how much does one name hold? Al-Baqir: had I found bearers for my knowledge, I would have spread tawhid, Islam, faith, religion and the laws from al-Samad (MM H5 P174:3). [SAME in content though not a packet point: B4 S11 step 5 teaches this line in full as its close of al-Samad (MM U4.12-2 P037:3); recall line, text printed again in the Teacher Copy, flagged. Ask 13]

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «قُلْ هُوَ اللَّهُ أَحَدٌ فَالْهَاءُ تَثْبِيتٌ لِلثَّابِتِ وَالْوَاوُ إِشَارَةٌ إِلَى الْغَائِبِ عَنْ دَرْكِ الْأَبْصَارِ وَلَمْسِ الْحَوَاسِّ» (al-Baqir; al-Tawhid, Bihar 3:222, al-Wafi 1:365; Jami FOUND; printed again, flagged, B6 S01).
  2. «الصَّمَدُ خَمْسَةُ أَحْرُفٍ ... وَاللَّامُ دَلِيلٌ عَلَى إِلَهِيَّتِهِ ... وَالْأَلِفُ وَاللَّامُ مُدْغَمَانِ لَا يَظْهَرَانِ عَلَى اللِّسَانِ وَلَا يَقَعَانِ فِي السَّمْعِ وَيَظْهَرَانِ فِي الْكِتَابَةِ» (al-Baqir via al-Sadiq, Wahb ibn Wahb; al-Tawhid, al-Wafi 1:367-368, Tafsir al-Safi 5:392-393; Jami FOUND as printed), cut to the alif and lam.
  3. «الصَّمَدُ الَّذِي لَا جَوْفَ لَهُ وَالصَّمَدُ الَّذِي قَدِ انْتَهَى سُؤْدُدُهُ وَالصَّمَدُ الَّذِي لَا يَأْكُلُ وَلَا يَشْرَبُ وَالصَّمَدُ الَّذِي لَا يَنَامُ وَالصَّمَدُ الدَّائِمُ» (al-Baqir from al-Sajjad from Imam Husayn; Bihar 3:223, al-Wafi 1:366; Jami FOUND; print the verified U8.5-5 P176:13 wording, not the unverified U8.5-2 P101:4).
  4. «الصَّمَدُ السَّيِّدُ الْمُطَاعُ الَّذِي لَيْسَ فَوْقَهُ آمِرٌ وَنَاهٍ» (al-Baqir; Bihar 3:223, al-Wafi 1:366; Jami FOUND), inline.
  5. «الَّذِي لَا شَرِيكَ لَهُ وَلَا يَئُودُهُ حِفْظُ شَيْءٍ وَلَا يَعْزُبُ عَنْهُ شَيْءٌ» (Imam Sajjad; Sharh al-Kafi 4:66, Kanz al-Daqa'iq 14:511; Jami FOUND; S12 also claims it, so in full here and recalled there).
  6. Imam Husayn's letter «وَإِنَّ اللَّهَ سُبْحَانَهُ قَدْ فَسَّرَ الصَّمَدَ فَقَالَ اللَّهُ أَحَدٌ اللَّهُ الصَّمَدُ ثُمَّ فَسَّرَهُ فَقَالَ لَمْ يَلِدْ وَلَمْ يُولَدْ» (Nawadir al-Akhbar 1:75-76, Kanz al-Daqa'iq 14:512; Jami FOUND; printed again, flagged, B4 S11; S12 also claims it).

Next lesson picks up

جس اللہ کو نہ حواس پاتے ہیں نہ فکر، عبادت میں ہم اسے کن لفظوں سے یاد کریں، اور «رب»، «رحمٰن» اور «رحیم» اس کے بارے میں کیا کہتے ہیں؟ (S07)

S07 «اللہ اکبر»، «سبحان اللہ» اور «الحمد للہ» بندگی میں اسی حیرت اور اسی پہچان کی آواز ہیں؛ اور سورۂ حمد میں «الحمد للہ» کے بعد رب، رحمان اور رحیم اس لیے آتے ہیں کہ وہی ساتھ رہ کر پالنے والا مالک ہے اور سب پر عام اور اپنوں پر خاص رحمت کرنے والا، اس لیے ہر ستائش کی جڑ وہی ہے

ledger H07 · 11 new · 10 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 2 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. How does the bewilderment of the last lessons speak in worship? «اللہ اکبر»: He is greater than to be described; the basis of takbir is that He is beyond description and of tasbih that He is pure of what they describe; He makes Himself known, «لم تجعل للخلق طريقاً إلى معرفتك إلا بالعجز عن معرفتك»; the Prophet's call, «أدعوكم إلى شهادة أن لا إله إلا الله» with the casting off of rivals, shut the door of tashbih at once; the narrations say «أكبر من أن يوصف»; and one word of the adhan falls on many of His attributes (MM U8.5-1 P061:4-5; AH P065:1-2, P063:1-9, P062:1-2 with P064:1-2, P065:5-6; JS P066:6-10 one line; EZ P067:1-10 one line, the order of the tasbih of al-Zahra; MM U8.5-3 P139:2-5 for the adhan; U8.5.10, U8.5.32). [U8.5.10 core repeat kept in full: recall B5 S04 "«اللہ اکبر» means He is greater than to be described" (first touched in B3 S01, Teacher Copy n25; also B4 S06, B5 S01 for the first call, B7 S03 step 7), core repeat · ask | U8.5.32 PART: recall B4 S06 "the adhan narration, read there for its la ilaha illa Allah line" and B7 S03 step 7 "its Allahu akbar block, one phrase holds many attributes", then new: MM's own frame, mosques as the place of the servant's mi'raj and the adhan as its announcement, and that the muezzin announces all those attributes each time he says it (P139:2, P139:3-5 last paragraph)]
  2. Who is the «اللہ» of that first call? The One every person already knows by fitra; the al- of «الله» is the al- of the innate covenant; and because what fitra finds holding us can be neither placed (above, below, inside, outside) nor denied, we are bewildered: so He is Allah, and He is akbar, and the du'a says «سبحانك اللهم وبحمدك لا أحصي ثناء عليك أنت كما أثنيت على نفسك» (AH P069:1-4 under the recall; AH P069:5-17 as the landing; EZ P070:13-28 one line under the recall; U8.5.11). ↺ B2 S16recall B2 S16 "the al- of «الله» is the al- of the innate covenant, the covenant He took in the world of dharr" | AH P069:5-17 is taught as the landing of step 1's core repeat, not as a new point
  3. Then why may we not describe Him by our own concepts? Describing Him by what He did not describe Himself with is defining Him by human concepts, which cannot even define knowledge and reason; so the takbirs that filled the world announce only His greatness above description and His purity above definition: «إن الخالق لا يوصف إلا بما وصف به نفسه» (Na'thal, one line, Ask) and «ولا تقدر عظمة الله سبحانه على قدر عقلك فتكون من الهالكين» (MM P068:2, P068:3 cut, P068:4-7 in two sentences, P068:10, P068:11; under U8.5.10). [Part of U8.5.10's core repeat: recall B5 S04 step 2 "our description does not reach Him, because every describer measures by his own scale", then MM's wording here, which Bab 1 to 6 did not print]
  4. What do «سبحان اللہ» and «الحمد للہ» add? He owns all things more than they own themselves and never leaves His rule, yet being defined is against His holiness: «الحمد لله» is His complete ownership, and «سبحان الله» removes any limit that ownership might suggest; «فسبح باسم ربك العظيم» (56:74); al-hamdu lillah affirms without ta'til, subhan Allah denies without tashbih (AH P071:1-15; JS P072:3-12, P072:18-29 as the recalled base, «واستخلص المجد والثناء» in Nahj's wording, Ask; JS U8.5-3 P147:1 one line; U8.5.12; U8.11.10 and U8.11.33 fine layer). [U8.5.12 PART: recall B1 S03 "all praise is His alone" (also B4 S15, the talbiya; B7 S28 step 5, every perfection and good is from Him, JS) then new: AH's al-hamdu lillah as His complete ownership, joined to subhan Allah, in AH P071:1-15 | U8.11.10 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B4 S10 "He the Owner, we the owned, from Ali's munajat" then new: JS's absolute Owner and absolutely owned, U8.11-1 P059:3-5 | U8.11.33 NEW, Teacher Copy: duas full of tasbih and tahmid have special greatness, JS U8.11-3 P113:1-8, Du'a al-'Asharat (P113:9-13, «اشراط» read as «عشرات», Ask), 110:1-3 one line]
  5. Why do Rabb, al-Rahman and al-Rahim follow «الحمد للہ» in al-Fatiha? Praise has four grounds: a thing is good in itself, or good reached us from it, or good is hoped from it, or its justice is feared; all four are in Him, and the names after «الله» name them in order: «الحمد لله» (all perfection is His), «رب العالمين» (He made and raises us), «الرحمن الرحيم» (we hope for His mercy), «مالك يوم الدين» (we fear His justice and praise Him for His grace); no name in that row is there without purpose, and this is why al-Rahman al-Rahim come again so soon after the basmala (JS U8.5-3 P144:2-4, P140:10-13, P141:8-21, P140:5-6, P140:14-36 with 1:2-4, P140:27-29, P140:37-40; the source scholar's name and book left out, P140:7-9, P141:4-7; U8.5.33). ↺ B7 S28Already taught: B7 S28 step 5 "praise is His because every perfection and every good is from Him"New here: the four grounds matched to the names of al-Fatiha, in JS P141:8-21 and P140:14-29
  6. What is the difference between al-Rahman and al-Rahim, and why did the name al-Rahman matter? Rahman is the mercy that reaches believer and unbeliever, the spread table where friend and foe sit; Rahim the special, lasting mercy for His friends; is the split this world and the next? one du'a says «يا رحمان الدنيا ورحيم الآخرة», another «يا رحمان الدنيا والآخرة ورحيمهما»: so general and special is common usage, not a rule without exception; Rahim is kindness, not only pardon (the rendering "the Bestower, the Forgiver" is wrong); and the polytheists scorned the name: al-Walid mocked "al-Rahman" as a man of Yamama, Suhayl at Hudaybiyya said «إنا لا نعرف الرحمن الرحيم», and the Quran reports «وما الرحمن» (25:60) (AH U8.5-2 P073:1-2, P073:3-4 and P073:6-7 under Ask 13; JS P074:6-17, P076:11 one image, P077:6-13, P080:3; EZ P083:4-9; EZ U8.5-4 P167:6-15; MM U8.5-2 P084:5-9; JS P085:4; EZ P086:1-7, P087:3-11 one line after verification; U8.5.13, U8.5.14, U8.5.45, U8.5.15). [U8.5.13 core repeat kept in full: recall B7 S23 step 4 "Rahman the general mercy for believer and unbeliever, Rahim the special for those who turn to Him" and B1 S08 "AH's two portions of 57:28 are the mercy of rahmaniyya and of rahimiyya", core repeat · ask | U8.5.14 PART: base recalled from B7 S23, new: the world split and its reconciliation, EZ P083:4-9, JS P077:6-13 | U8.5.45 PART: base recalled from B7 S23, new: EZ's critique of the rendering and Rahim as kindness, U8.5-4 P167:6-15 | U8.5.15 NEW]
  7. What is Rabb? Not every owner of a garden is its rabb: the Arabs say «رب البستان» only of the one who stays with it, waters, prunes and harvests; the one who only owns and leases it is its malik or sahib; so not every maker, creator or owner is rabb: the rabb stays with what he raises until it reaches its perfection, and perfect raising means He knows, can, is kind and is wise: «ربنا الذي أعطى كل شيء خلقه ثم هدى» (20:50); and so the soul at rest is told «ارجعي إلى ربك» (89:28), to its Rabb, not to a court (EZ U8.5-2 P107:13-35, P108:3-22, P110:8-17, one image; JS P106:4-8, P111:5-8, P112:13-17; EZ U8.5-3 P123:2 with 20:50; EZ U8.5-4 P162:6-9, P163, 89:27-28 once; U8.5.23, U8.5.41; fine layer U8.5.17, .18, .40, .42, .43, .44). [U8.5.23 NEW | U8.5.41 NEW | the fine-layer PART points U8.5.18, .40, .42 recalled in the Teacher Copy, see Repeats]
  8. Close: what does a servant gain by «الحمد للہ»? Al-Sadiq offered a man a dhikr that makes every one who prays pray for him: «الحمد لله»; for every worshipper says «سمع الله لمن حمده», "Lord, hear (accept) the one who praises You"; so praise that names Him rightly is itself answered, and the next lesson asks why every sura and every act opens with His name (JS U8.5-3 P146:1-18, P147:7-17; EZ* EFB_07_S03 ¶189 as the confirming source; U8.5.34). new

Who carries it

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «أَكْبَرُ مِنْ أَنْ يُوصَفَ» (al-Sadiq; al-Kafi via Tafsir al-Safi 3:228-229, Kanz al-Daqa'iq 7:543; Jami FOUND; printed in full in B5 S04) with «لم تجعل للخلق طريقاً إلى معرفتك إلا بالعجز عن معرفتك» (Munajat al-'Arifin, Bihar 91:150-151, a late collection; FOUND).
  2. «وَلَا تُقَدِّرْ عَظَمَةَ اللهِ سُبْحَانَهُ عَلَى قَدْرِ عَقْلِكَ فَتَكُونَ مِنَ الْهَالِكِينَ» (Amir al-Mu'minin, Nahj, Khutbat al-Ashbah; FOUND) and «إِنَّ الْخَالِقَ لَا يُوصَفُ إِلَّا بِمَا وَصَفَ بِهِ نَفْسَهُ» (the Prophet to Na'thal; Bihar 3:304, al-Durr al-Nazim 1:789; FOUND; Ask 4).
  3. «كنا جلوسا في المسجد إذ صعد المؤذن المنارة فقال الله أكبر ...» (Imam Husayn telling Amir al-Mu'minin's commentary on the adhan; Ma'ani al-Akhbar and al-Tawhid, Mustadrak al-Wasa'il 4:65-70, Bihar 81:131; FOUND; the printed block starts with Saduq's chain).
  4. «الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ الرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِيمِ مَالِكِ يَوْمِ الدِّينِ» (1:2-4, JS U8.5-3 P140:36).
  5. «يَا رَحْمَانَ الدُّنْيَا وَرَحِيمَ الْآخِرَةِ» (al-Rida, prayer for a need; Bihar 88:355, Mustadrak 6:319-320; FOUND) beside «يا رحمان الدنيا والآخرة ورحيمهما» (Muhaj al-Da'awat p.475, al-Misbah of al-Kaf'ami p.174; FOUND).
  6. «وَلَكِنَّهُ كَلَامُ اللّهِ الَّذِي بَعَثَ أَنْبِيَاءَهُ وَرُسُلَهُ ... فَلَمَّا سَمِعَ الرَّحْمَنَ اسْتَهْزَأَ» (the Prophet and al-Walid; I'lam al-Wara 1:110-113, Bihar 17:211-212; FOUND) with «وَإِذَا قِيلَ لَهُمُ اسْجُدُوا لِلرَّحْمَٰنِ قَالُوا وَمَا الرَّحْمَٰنُ» (25:60); Suhayl's «إنا لا نعرف الرحمن الرحيم» (al-Ihtijaj p.443; FOUND-OTHER-WORDING for the printed lines).
  7. «قَالَ رَبُّنَا الَّذِي أَعْطَىٰ كُلَّ شَيْءٍ خَلْقَهُ ثُمَّ هَدَىٰ» (20:50) and «ارْجِعِي إِلَىٰ رَبِّكِ رَاضِيَةً مَرْضِيَّةً» (89:28); the al-Sadiq «الحمد لله» story of step 8 is NOT-FOUND in Jami (told in Urdu only; JS's source needed, Ask 20).
Next lesson picks up

ہر سورہ اور ہر کام اللہ کے نام سے کیوں شروع ہوتا ہے، اور «بسم اللہ» کہنے والا اپنے بارے میں کیا کہتا ہے؟ (S08)

4. Bismillah S08 to S08

S08 بسم اللہ کہنے والا اپنے اوپر خدا کی بندگی کی مہر لگاتا ہے؛ یہ کلمہ خود خدا نے سکھایا، یہ شیطان کو دور رکھتا ہے، اور اس کا اثر ادب اور بندگی کے ساتھ کہنے میں ہے، صرف زبان پر لانے میں نہیں

ledger · 17 new · 4 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Why does every sura, and every act, open with God's name? Because God Himself set it: in the Mi'raj prayer He said «الآن وصلت إلي فسمّ باسمي», so bismillah heads the suras; and every matter of weight not begun with it is «أبتر», cut off; Sulayman's letter is the Quranic model: it is from Sulayman but opens with God's name, not his (MM H2 P076:1, with H1 P001:1-5 merged as one line; «قطعت ذكري» P076:3 one line at most; AH H1 P012:1-2; MM* MMB_10_S12 ¶537 «وإلّا فهو أبتر لا خير فيه» if usable; JS H1 P013:8-10 one line; EZ/JS H1 P038:19-22 with 27:30, 27:29 and 27:31 folded into prose; U8.6.18, U8.6.4, U8.6.8). new
  2. What does a person say when he says bismillah? He sets on himself a mark from God's marks, and that mark is servitude (al-Rida, «أسم على نفسي سمة من سمات الله ... وهي العبادة»); «سمة» is from «وسم»; the common readings (I recite, I begin, I seek help) stand in one line, with God's word «أي أستعين على هذا الأمر بالله»; the controlling image is the branded horse that thieves see and pass by (JS H2 P068:6, P068:10-12, P067:3, P070:10-15, P057:17-29; JS H1 P019:6 one line; U8.6.12; U8.6.14 the annexation of «بسم» as a teacher note only if kept). [U8.6.12 NEW; the «أستعين» line touches U8.6.6, PART: see step 3]
  3. Why the "name", and not simply "by God"? The Essence is beyond every faculty, so we point to it by the name, a mirror that is fani in the Named; there is courtesy in it too, as a centre is opened in a great person's name; so in truth the help is from God Himself, and we are told to say the name because it shows the Named; «بالله» is also said, and both stand together in the Mi'raj formula «بسم الله وبالله ... والأسماء الحسنى كلها لله» and the mosque du'a «بسم الله وبالله ... وخير الأسماء كلها لله» (JS H1 P004:4-17, P022:9-22, P021:13-17, P040:5-11 with 87:1; EZ H1 P025:13-17, P034:1 as the one corroborating line, God taught «بسم الله», not «بالله»; MM H2 P076:8 and H1 P001:1-5; U8.11-1 JS P058:5-8; U8.6.2, U8.6.10, U8.6.1, U8.6.6, U8.11.9; U8.6.9 «بالله» in a du'a, one teacher line at most). [U8.6.1 PART: recall B1 S10 "MM: «بسم الله وبالله ولا حول ولا قوة إلا بالله» recurs in the du'as because finding the Lord is the witness to His Messenger", then new: the formula's setting in the Mi'raj and its reading as the name and the Named (MM H1 P001:1-5, EZ H1 P002:6-9); U8.6.6 PART: recall B2 S01 "al-'Askari: «بسم الله» means I seek help in all my affairs from God, to whom every creature turns when all means are cut", then new: whether the help is from the name or from God Himself (JS H1 P022:18-22, EZ H1 P025:11-24), taught as one reading, help from God sought through the name He set, if Ali allows (Q2); U8.6.2, U8.6.10, U8.11.9 NEW]
  4. What does God's name do for the one who says it? It keeps Satan off: the angels strike the devils' faces, «ما سبيلكم عليه وقد سمّى الله»; an angel is set over each name, and where the angel is the devil does not come; this lands the brand of step 2: bismillah works like «أعوذ بالله» (MM H1 P015:27 once, cut to «وقد سمّى الله»; AH H1 P016:5-8; JS H2 P057:30 to P058:4; EZ H1 P018:25-27 the jinn line, fine layer; U8.6.5). [NEW; ledger layer teacher: see Ask 6]
  5. And if it is left out? Satan has a share in that wudu, food, drink or clothing; and a believer who leaves it may be reminded by a trial: the man who fell from the stool before Ali, and the man made to repeat his wudu until Ali told him to say bismillah (MM H2 P075:9 with P075:8 one line; JS H2 P073:6-16; JS H1 P013:19-44; MM H2 P075:2 the intercourse report, Teacher Copy only, once; EZ H1 P014:9-15 with 6:121, first clause, only if a second example is wanted; U8.6.17, U8.6.15, U8.6.4). new
  6. Is it the words alone that count? No: the great reward is for saying it «بالحرمة والتعظيم»; and its letters hold more than words show: al-Sadiq, «الباء بهاء الله والسين سناء الله والميم مجد الله»; Ali spoke on the ba alone until dawn; and it is nearer the Greatest Name than the black of the eye to its white (JS H2 P074:2-3, P074:5-7, P074:9-11, without the houri block; JS H2 P055:1 first sentence, its second half to S07; AH H2 P077:1-8, P077:16-28, P077:35-37 or the re-read ZH-S26 ¶43-45, one telling; JS H1 P043:10 one line pointing to S16; U8.6.16, U8.6.13, U8.6.19, U8.6.11). [U8.6.16, U8.6.13, U8.6.11 NEW; U8.6.19 PART: recall B1 S12 "AH: Ibn Abbas sat a long night with Ali, the ocean rose wave on wave, and dawn came with his words on the ba unfinished", then new: the story read for the depth of the basmala itself, AH's own new wording at H2 P077:26-28 and P077:35-37 ("even one ba has this much to say; this is one window of it")]
  7. Then does whoever speaks in God's name stand with the truth? No: Umar ibn Sa'd sent his army out with "in the name of God", and rulers ruled under titles "bi-llah"; the name must be said with the servitude it marks (EZ H2 P078:1-14, P079:4-7 cut to the point; bridge from step 2 as a one-line recall of S02: EZ, the tongue and hand are God's names in trust, so saying bismillah and then lying is misuse of the name, H1 P010:21-28, P010:35-36, taught in S02; U8.6.20, U8.6.21). [U8.6.20 PART: recall B1 S02 "EZ: the one who straps on a bomb to kill acts with intent of nearness, so intention without true knowledge of God is not enough", then new: Umar ibn Sa'd's cry and the misuse of God's name, EZ H2 P078:1-14; U8.6.21 NEW, Teacher Copy]

Arabic printed again

6:121 «وَلَا تَأْكُلُوا مِمَّا لَمْ يُذْكَرِ اسْمُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ» (printed again, flagged "already printed in full in B1 S07", only if EZ's eating example stays; also inline in B2 S19; Bab 7 is not written yet). Checked and not the same text, so printed normally: the 'Ilal Mi'raj report (table row U8.6-2 P076): B1 S07 prints its «سمع الله لمن حمده» clause, B4 S11 its «اقرأ قل هو الله أحد ... نسبتي ونعتي» clause, B6 S07 its «فانظر إلى عرشي» clause; the clauses here («الآن وصلت إلي فسمّ باسمي», «قطعت ذكري», «بسم الله وبالله ... والأسماء الحسنى كلها لله») are not printed in any earlier lesson; note "same report, other clauses: B1 S07, B4 S11, B6 S07". The al-'Askari / ship report row (U8.6-1 P019): B2 S10 prints the clause «يقول بسم الله أي أستعين على أموري كلها بالله» and the ship report; the clause here is God's word «فقولوا عند افتتاح كل أمر صغير أو عظيم ... أي أستعين على هذا الأمر بالله», another sentence of the same report; note "parallel clause in B2 S10". The row «بسم الله وبالله ومن الله وإلى الله وخير الأسماء كلها» (U8.6-1 P015:27, U8.6-2 P075:1, U8.11-1 P058:6) is listed as printed in full in B1 S05, B2 S07, B4 S11, B5 S08, but a vowel-free search of those lessons finds neither the mosque du'a nor the leaving-house text; the formula «بسم الله وبالله» is only inline in B1 S10 (and B1 S09); note "inline B1 S10" (Ask 20).

Who carries it

JS carries steps 2, 3 and 6 and the stories of step 5 (about half the Fasl's spans); MM carries step 1 (the Mi'raj origin) and the texts of steps 4 and 5 («وقد سمّى الله», the wudu share); AH carries the «أبتر» hadith (step 1), the angel on each name (step 4) and Ali's night on the ba (step 6); EZ adds the corroborating lines of steps 1, 3 and 5. Step 7 is EZ alone: AH and MM are absent there. AH and MM are thin across the lesson (about 20% of spans, diag 1); MM* MMB_10_S12 ¶537 and AH SH-S03 ¶98 (the formula for the tashahhud, editor's footnote) are named sources not in the gathers (Q1).

Moves inside the Bab by the ledger, kept flagged here until Ali rules (Ask 21): H1 P016:1-3, P016:9-11, P017 (EZ's counts, «یا رب» three times) and P002:1-13 (asking by intermediaries, the Rajab du'a) to S17; H1 P041 to P051 bar one line, and P050:7-9 (nineteen times), to S16; H2 P055:1 second half, P069, P071:14-24, P072:7-8 to S07; H1 P006, P010 (created realities and our limbs as names, U8.6.3) to S02.

Texts and Jami results

«الْآنَ وَصَلْتَ إِلَيَّ فَسَمِّ بِاسْمِي فَقَالَ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ فَمِنْ أَجْلِ ذَلِكَ جُعِلَ ... فِي أَوَّلِ السُّورَةِ» ('Ilal al-shara'i', Bihar 79:241; MM H2 P076:1; Jami found: Kanz al-Daqa'iq 1:18); «كُلُّ أَمْرٍ ذِي بَالٍ لَمْ يُذْكَرْ فِيهِ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ فَهُوَ أَبْتَرُ» (AH H1 P012:1-2, inline, no reference; Jami: Bihar 89:242 from Tafsir al-Imam; Wasa'il no. 9032 reads «لا يذكر بسم الله فيه»); «مَعْنَى قَوْلِ الْقَائِلِ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ أَيْ أَسِمُ عَلَى نَفْسِي سِمَةً مِنْ سِمَاتِ اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ وَهِيَ الْعِبَادَةُ» (Ma'ani al-akhbar 3-4; JS H2 P068:6; Jami found; JS's inline «ارسم نفسي بسمة الله» is his paraphrase, not Jami wording); «فَقُولُوا عِنْدَ افْتِتَاحِ كُلِّ أَمْرٍ صَغِيرٍ أَوْ عَظِيمٍ بِسْمِ اللهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ أَيْ أَسْتَعِينُ عَلَى هَذَا الْأَمْرِ بِاللهِ» (al-Tawhid, Nur al-thaqalayn 1:12-13; JS H1 P019:6; Jami found); «فَتَضْرِبُ الْمَلَائِكَةُ وُجُوهَهَا وَتَقُولُ مَا سَبِيلُكُمْ عَلَيْهِ وَقَدْ سَمَّى اللَّهَ» (al-Kafi 2:511-512; MM H1 P015:27 = H2 P075:1, one text); «إِذَا تَوَضَّأَ أَحَدُكُمْ وَلَمْ يُسَمِّ كَانَ لِلشَّيْطَانِ فِي وُضُوئِهِ شِرْكٌ» (al-Mahasin, Bihar 60:203; MM H2 P075:9; Jami found); «الْبَاءُ بَهَاءُ اللَّهِ وَالسِّينُ سَنَاءُ اللَّهِ وَالْمِيمُ مَجْدُ اللَّهِ» (al-Kafi 1; JS H2 P055:1; the printed excerpt is right, JS's spoken line says «ثناء» for Jami's «سناء»). Also in the steps: 27:30; «تَرَكَ بَعْضُ شِيعَتِنَا فِي افْتِتَاحِ أَمْرِهِ بِسْمِ اللّٰهِ ... فَيَمْتَحِنُهُ اللّٰهُ بِمَكْرُوهٍ» (Bihar 73:305; JS H2 P073:6); the reverence report «... لِمَنْ يَقُولُ بِالْحُرْمَةِ وَالتَّعْظِيمِ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمنِ الرَّحِيمِ» (Bihar 89:258, from Jami' al-akhbar, a late source); «بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمنِ الرَّحِيمِ أَقْرَبُ إِلَى اسْمِ اللَّهِ الْأَعْظَمِ مِنْ نَاظِرِ الْعَيْنِ إِلَى بَيَاضِهَا» (al-Tahdhib, Kanz al-Daqa'iq 1:17; JS H1 P043:10); the mosque du'a «بِسْمِ اللَّهِ وَبِاللَّهِ وَمِنَ اللَّهِ وَإِلَى اللَّهِ وَخَيْرُ الْأَسْمَاءِ كُلِّهَا لِلَّهِ» (Misbah al-mutahajjid 32-33; U8.11-1 JS P058:5-8).

Next lesson picks up

اللہ کو آسمانوں اور زمین کا نور کیوں کہا گیا، اور اس نام کا معنی کیا ہے؟ (Why is God called the Light of the heavens and the earth, and what does that name mean?) (S09)

5. The names one by one: light, the four names, power, pardon, beauty and majesty S09 to S14

S09 «اللہ آسمانوں اور زمین کا نور ہے، یعنی ان میں رہنے والوں کا ہادی؛ اس کا نور اس کا اپنا ہے اور ہر دوسرا نور اسی سے ہے»

ledger H09 · 9 new · 8 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Is "light" said of God in the sense it has for a lamp? No: God is not sensed, pictured or imagined, so the light of the verse is not the light the eye sees; the name is shared, the meaning is not. (JS U8.7-1 P008:1-4; within the Bab, one recall line of S03: "the name is one, the meaning is different"). [Bab recall: S03 carries the principle and its texts (al-Rida's «جمعنا الاسم ولم يجمعنا المعنى» has its home in S03 or S10, not here); JS P008:1-4 is the lead-in of U8.7.1]
  2. What is light? What shows itself and shows others; the lamp needs no proof, "the sun came as proof of the sun"; so God is known by God and all else by Him, more manifest than anything; He is the source of the light of existence, life, knowledge and power, and what is nearer the source has the stronger light. (AH P004:1-11, P005:3-10, P005:18-22 block cut to «متی غبت... عمیت عین لا تراک»; AH P006:1-5; EZ P017:1-4, P018:3-7 one line each as corroboration; U8.7.2, part of U8.7.3; U8.7.4 teacher line). [U8.7.2 PART: recall B1 S08 "knowledge is a light manifest by itself that manifests others" (and B3 S14 for reason), then new: the definition said of God, existence, life and knowledge as lights whose source is God, in AH P004:11, P005:9-10, P006:1-5 | U8.7.4 PART (Teacher Copy): same base; new only the Ishraqi report, one teacher line, no names]
  3. Then what does «اللہ نور السماوات والارض» mean? Guide of the dwellers of the heavens and of the earth: al-Rida's answer, and the same in his debate with Imran; He guides by reason, by the prophets, and by the Seal of the Prophets and the Imams. (JS P008:5-9 with the Kafi block «هاد لأهل السماء وهاد لأهل الأرض»; MM P003:1-13 inline, P009:8 / P002:2 the Barqi wording one line; JS P008:10-14, P014:5-6; U8.7.1). [U8.7.1 core repeat kept in full (see Repeats); new: al-Rida's gloss "Guide of the people of heaven and earth", which neither B1 S08 nor B1 S16 prints | RR8-082 SAME: recalled under this step]
  4. Why not "existence" or "giver of being"? The verse's second half («مثل نوره») keeps the same light, so cutting the verse in two does not hold; light as existence has no ground in the lexicon or in the Ma'sumin's words; bringing into being would not be limited to heaven and earth; and the witr qunut calls Him "the adornment and the beauty of the heavens and earth", which already supposes them made. (JS P013:1-2, P012:1-13; U8.7-4 JS P199:2, P201:4-6, P202:2, P200:2-3 for .40; P203:1-2, P201:1-3, P204:1-3 for .41; P205:1-5 for .42; P206:2 for .43; all in about four sentences, no names; U8.7.38, .39, .44 fine layer). [U8.7.40 PART: recall B1 S09 "God is not existence; existence is a light owned by the King" (and B2 S09), then new: 24:35 read against the "existence" reading, in JS P199:2, P201:4-6, P202:2 | U8.7.41 NEW | U8.7.42 NEW | U8.7.43 NEW | U8.7.38, .39, .44 NEW (Teacher Copy)]
  5. What is the parable? His guidance lit in the believer's heart like a lamp in a niche in a dark room: niche, lamp, glass, pure oil, "light upon light". (JS P019:1-24, P007:7, P007:11-13, P015:4-22; EZ P020:1 one line). [part of the U8.7.1 core repeat: recall B1 S08 and B1 S16 "«مثل نوره مثل هداه في قلب المؤمن»" and B3 S02 "JS's dark room with a lamp in the niche: the light of guidance in a heart without aim or illness"; JS's repeat sits under the recall, flagged | RR8-043 SAME: recalled under this step]
  6. Is any other light like His? No: the sun's light is lent and is taken back each day, and one day the sun is folded up; His light is His own (EZ P020:12-20 with 81:1-2 inline, basmala dropped, Suhrawardi unnamed; AH P005:6 «آفتاب آمد دلیل آفتاب» one line). 6b. And light has degrees with no limit at either end; before the light of «یا سبوح یا قدوس» our sun is a small wick lamp (AH U8.7-5 P244:1-19, the maxim «عدم الوجدان...» in Urdu, two sentences). [U8.7.3 PART: recall B3 S04 "nothing is more manifest than God", then new: the light of the heavens is not the sun's but His own, the sun's lent, in EZ P020:12-20 and AH P005:6 | U8.7.64 PART: recall B3 S17 "a graded nature has no limit at either end (AH)", then new: the rule applied to the light of «یا سبوح یا قدوس», in AH P244:12-19]
  7. How do the du'as of the Ahl al-Bayt call Him light? Fatima's hirz «بسم الله النور، بسم الله نور النور... الذي خلق النور من النور»: the Light of light is God Himself, and she had it from the Prophet, not from a philosophy; and «یا نور یا قدوس»: our lights are limited and smoky, His light is holy. (EZ U8.7-1 P018:9-24 cut to the light lines, merged with U8.7-4 P208:31-39 into one block; EZ P208:27-32, P024:8 one line each; EZ P210:7-11; U8.7.45 teacher line). [U8.7.5 NEW | U8.7.46 NEW | U8.7.45 NEW (Teacher Copy)]

  • ﴿اللَّهُ نُورُ السَّمَاوَاتِ وَالْأَرْضِ ۚ مَثَلُ نُورِهِ كَمِشْكَاةٍ...﴾ (24:35 whole, the Fasl prints it once, at EZ U8.7-1 P020:21; the copies at JS U8.7-3 P194:1 and U8.7-4 P207:1-2 are not printed again): already printed in full in B1 S08 and B3 S02 (inline in B1 S11). Who carries it

AH carries step 2 (P004, P005, P006) and step 6 (P005:6, P244); MM gives step 3 its second al-Rida line (P003, Imran) beside JS's Kafi block. JS carries steps 1, 3, 4 and 5 (P008, P012 to P015, P019, P007, U8.7-4 P199 to P206); EZ gives step 6 (P020) and the whole of step 7 (P018, P208, P210). Steps 4, 5 and 7 have no AH or MM: the U8.7-4 critique has no AH or MM at all (diag U8.7-a section 1); step 5's AH record (RR8-043) is SAME; for step 7 the point text names AH* reciting the same Fatima du'a (QJ-S04 ¶40-41, R:E17.2b), not gathered (Ask 7).

Imports

none; S09 is not a thin lesson (the packet has no import rows).
