Next lesson picks up

اول، آخر، ظاہر اور باطن، لطیف اور سلام جیسے نام اللہ کے لیے کس معنی میں ہیں، جب یہی الفاظ مخلوق کے لیے بھی بولے جاتے ہیں؟ (S10)

S10 اللہ اول و آخر، ظاہر و باطن، لطیف، سلام اور حق ہے، مگر مخلوق کے معنی میں نہیں: وہ ازلی و ابدی ہے، اپنی قدرت اور علم سے ہر چیز کو گھیرے ہوئے ہے، بے اوزار اور بے مادہ لطیف چیزیں بنانے والا ہے، خود سلامتی ہے، اور وہ ثابت حقیقت ہے جس سے ہر حق پرکھا جاتا ہے

ledger H10 · 8 new · 5 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 2 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. اول اور آخر کا کیا معنی ہے؟ God glosses the names Himself in the Mi'raj report: «أنا الأول ولا شيء قبلي، وأنا الآخر فلا شيء بعدي، وأنا الظاهر فلا شيء فوقي، وأنا الباطن فلا شيء تحتي» (MM U8.7-1 P027:6-9, block cut to God's own gloss; 57:3 block once, JS P029:7-8); so First is pre-eternal and Last everlasting, not the first in a row of beings that came out of nothing (JS P029:24-29, P030:15-18); al-Sadiq to Ibn Abi Ya'fur on "the Last": everything perishes or changes from colour to colour, state to state, except the Lord of the worlds (JS P040:1-3 as a block with reference; MM* has the same hadith, MMB_07_S03 ¶117). (U8.7.6, U8.7.7) [U8.7.6 CORE REPEAT kept in full, recall B3 S03 / B4 S11 noted; new: MM's report "nothing before Me ... nothing above Me", JS's "not first in a row" | U8.7.7 PART: recall B4 S11 "the Last is He who remains when all perishes" then new: al-Sadiq's hadith, JS P040:2-3]
  2. ظاہر اور باطن کا کیا معنی ہے؟ Manifest by His signs and dominant over everything; hidden from the imaginations; inward to things not by entering them, as oil in a vessel or water in fruit, but by knowledge, keeping and governance (JS U8.7-4 P213:1-13 with 61:14 inline, P215:1-4, P216:10-15; JS U8.7-1 P037:1-16, P038:4-13); AH: His hiddenness is within His very self-disclosure, so whoever calls Him «یا غیب» calls Him at the same moment «یا ظاہر» (AH U8.7-1 P028:1-4); the al-Rida line «جمعنا الاسم واختلف المعنى» recalled in one line from S03, where it is taught in full. (U8.7.6, U8.7.47, U8.7.48; RR8-058) [U8.7.6 core part: new AH P028:2-4 and "manifest by His signs" | U8.7.47 SAME: recall only | U8.7.48 SAME: recall only | RR8-058 SAME: recall only]
  3. یہ چاروں نام آپس میں کیسے جڑے ہیں؟ All four are branches of His encompassing: whatever is supposed first, He is before it; whatever last, He is after it; whatever manifest, He is more manifest, whatever hidden, more hidden; not in time or place; His power and His knowledge surround everything (JS U8.7-4 P217:1-8, P219:11-29, P221:1-7, P222:8-10 with 4:126 inline; short, no names). (U8.7.49) ↺ B3 S03Already taught: B3 S03 "the meanings of the four names of 57:3"New here: all four as branches of al-Muhit, P217, P219, P221
  4. کیا یہ الفاظ کسی مخلوق کے لیے بھی آ سکتے ہیں؟ Yes, in another sense: the same Mi'raj report names Ali First and Last, and itself explains it: first of the Imams whose covenant was taken, last whose spirit is taken; manifest as the one to whom all revelation was shown, hidden as the keeper of the secret (MM U8.7-1 P042:1-3, one line; JS* SYB_01_S02 ¶256-262 corroborates). One line only, ASK whether it stays (U8.7-a Q4). (U8.7.8, U8.7.9) new
  5. لطیف کا کیا معنی ہے؟ Not the craftsman's fine work, nor smallness or thinness: He is called Latif because He created the subtle creatures and knows the subtle thing (Fath b. Yazid hadith, MM U8.7-1 P043 Latif lines; JS P044:7-23, P055:6-23); our subtlety is in shaping material with tools, His is without tool and from no material (al-Rida: «إن خالقنا لطيف لا كلطف خلقه في صنعته», JS P053:3-4, P054:7-8, P052:12-19; JS P064:9 to P065:4); «وأما اللطيف فليس على قلة وقضافة وصغر» (JS P048:2); «إن ربي لطيف اللطافة فلا يوصف باللطف» (JS P062:8-9, the line only). (U8.7.10, U8.7.11, U8.7.50, U8.7.51) [U8.7.10 CORE REPEAT kept in full: recall B3 S09 noted; new: "without treatment or instrument" | U8.7.11 PART: recall B3 S09 "Latif for His subtle creation and His knowledge of the subtle" then new: JS's fuller account of the senses, P048:2, P062:8-9, the rest fine layer | U8.7.50 NEW, Teacher Copy | U8.7.51 PART, Teacher Copy]
  6. سلام کا کیا معنی ہے؟ From Him peace comes and to Him it returns (MM U8.7-1 P067:1-9 or P068:1-11, one block; Khadija's reply «إن الله هو السلام ومنه السلام وإليه السلام», JS P071:10-15, one line at most, ASK); He is sound from every defect, and all creatures are safe from Him; no harm comes from Him (JS P072:2-10, P072:17-21). The Night of Qadr polemic (EZ U8.7-5 P233) sits after this step, Teacher Copy only, ASK. (U8.7.12, U8.7.13, U8.7.55) [U8.7.12 NEW | U8.7.13 PART: recall B5 S02 "God is free of every defect" then new: the name al-Salam, the one from whom all are safe, JS P072:5-10 | U8.7.55 NEW, Teacher Copy, ASK]
  7. «سالم» کی جگہ «سلام» اور «عادل» کی جگہ «عدل» کیوں؟ Some names are masdars; a masdar is not said of a person except by a device, and the strongest is hyperbole; hyperbole about creatures can be a lie, but He truly is justice itself, soundness itself, truth itself (EZ U8.7-1 P073:2-9, P073:85-102, P073:126-137, P074:19-21, P075:17-25 merged with EZ U8.7-4 P227:1-18, P228:4-8). (U8.7.52, U8.7.13) [U8.7.52 NEW | U8.7.13 new part: al-Salam as a masdar, God soundness itself]
  8. حق کا کیا معنی ہے؟ Haqq is the fixed, whose being never changes; what came to be after not being, or will pass, is not Haqq absolutely; God alone is Haqq absolutely, and every other right is measured by His and told by Him (EZ U8.7-5 P232:6-16, P229:1-7, P229:14-15, P230:1-4; one ayah once its ref is settled, U8.7-b q19); al-Haqq among His comprehensive essential names (EZ P229:8-12, P230:9-11, Teacher Copy). (U8.7.54, U8.7.53) new

Not the same text, printed normally: the Mi'raj report (MM P027:6-9) shares only a 59:23 run inside a long matn; al-Rida's 'Uyun report is printed in B2 S09 by another line («بصير لا كبصر خلقه»), not by its Latif line; Saduq's gloss on al-Zahir (U8.7-4 P213:2) appears in B3 S09 only inside an ASK.

Who carries it

MM carries step 1 (God's own gloss of the four names), step 4 (the names said of Ali), the ground of step 5 (the Fath b. Yazid hadith) and step 6 (al-Salam in the reports). AH has one passage (step 2, P028:1-4, hiddenness within self-disclosure) and the lamp record, which is SAME and recalled only. JS carries steps 2 and 3 and most of 5 (al-Rida's reports). EZ alone carries steps 7 and 8: AH and MM are absent from steps 3, 7 and 8 (the AH holder of U8.7.53 was not gathered; AH's and MM's Haqq hits are other contexts; AH HLB_01_S11 ¶1739 on al-Muhit not gathered).

AH re-read adds

RR8-058 (U8.7, AZ-S11 ¶17-33, the lamp, light nearer to the lamp than the lamp to itself, as a model of His encompassing presence; SAME, recalled only, step 2).

Imports

none (S10 is not a thin lesson).

Teacher Copy only

Texts and Jami results

Yazid «وأما الباطن فليس على معنى الاستبطان للأشياء ... علماً وحفظاً وتدبيراً» with «جمعنا الاسم واختلف المعنى» (al-Kafi; Jami FOUND; the line taught in full in S03); the Fath b. Yazid Latif hadith «إنما قلنا اللطيف للخلق اللطيف ولعلمه بالشيء اللطيف» (al-Kafi, al-Wafi 1:481; Jami FOUND; B3 S09 reprint, flagged); al-Rida «إن خالقنا لطيف لا كلطف خلقه في صنعته» ('Uyun 1, bab 11; Jami FOUND by its shorter stretch) and «وأما اللطيف فليس على قلة وقضافة وصغر» (al-Kafi; Jami FOUND, «قضافة» for the recording's «قذافة»); al-Salam: Khadija «إن الله هو السلام ومنه السلام وإليه السلام» (Tafsir al-'Ayyashi, Nur al-Thaqalayn 3:133; Jami FOUND), MM's forms only in Jami's wording «هو السلام ومنه السلام وإليه يعود السلام» (FOUND-OTHER-WORDING as printed); the Haqq ayah: open (U8.7-b q19).

Next lesson picks up

اللہ کے اتنے بہت سے نام کیوں ہیں، اور ہر نام اس کے کمال کی کس جہت کو دکھاتا ہے؟ (S11)

S11 «اللہ کے کمالات بے شمار ہیں اور اس کا ہر نام اس کے ایک کمال کی جہت دکھاتا ہے: پیدا کرنے کے نام خلقت کے الگ الگ مرحلے بتاتے ہیں، جلال اور قدرت کے نام اس کی بے مثال بادشاہی، اور قرآن ہر موقع پر وہی نام چنتا ہے جو اس جگہ کا حق ہے»

ledger H11 · 7 new · 14 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why does God have so many names, if He is one? His perfections and manifestations have no limit; at least a thousand and one show in this world, each with its name; so each name looks at Him from one aspect of perfection, the aspect by which we know Him, and no two names are synonyms (AH U8.7-6 P246:1-24, P246:31-34; AH U8.7-2 P105:4-6; MM U8.7-2 P104:4, P104:49-51; Teacher Copy: AH U8.3-1 P118:2-6, P119:4-7 Karim and Jawad, Muhsin and Mujmil, EZ U8.3-1 P029; points U8.7.21, U8.7.66, U8.3.14). [NEW: U8.7.21. PART: U8.7.66 recall B7 S05 "AH: death and the grave are a manifestation of His subduing (qahhariyya)" then new: Ad, Thamud and Pharaoh as manifestations of compulsion and the people of Yunus as a manifestation of kindness, AH U8.7-6 P246:25-30, told once for the Fasl. PART: U8.3.14 recall B7 S23 "Rahman is the general mercy, Rahim the special" then new: the rule that no two names are synonyms, AH P118:1-2, P119:4-7, opponent unnamed, Teacher Copy]
  2. Then is each name in the Quran chosen for its place? Yes: the people of the ditch were burned for believing in God «العزیز الحمید», not «الرحمن الرحیم» nor «الغفور الغفار»: the Mighty who could have stopped it, the Praised whose every act is right; in the Quran even a waw or a fa is the fittest choice (JS U8.7-7 P272:8-17, P268:11-18, P270:13-17; 85:5 to 85:7 as one line; Teacher Copy: al-Jumu'a 1 «الملک القدوس العزیز الحکیم», JS P267:13-15, basmala dropped; points U8.7.83, U8.7.82). ↺ B4 S17Already taught: U8.7.83 recall B4 S17 "JS: the people of the ditch were burned only for believing in the One God, the Mighty, the Praised, and His respite was praiseworthy"New here: the contrast with Rahman, Rahim, Ghafur, Ghaffar and the rule that every name and every letter of the Quran is chosen, JS P272:16-17, P270:13-17. NEW: U8.7.82, Teacher Copy
  3. How do the names of creating differ? Persian has a handful of words for God; Arabic has ten or twelve for His creating alone, each from one aspect: Fatir splits the first matter (35:1), Faliq splits and brings out (Ali's oath «فلق الحب وبرأ النسمة»), Khaliq makes from a given matter, Bari' shapes and evens and is maker of the bariyya, Musawwir gives form (59:24); Sani' only reshapes what exists, so the carpenter is a maker and never a creator; no maker makes as He makes (EZ U8.7-6 P251:12-17, P252:6-18 as the opening line, Teacher Copy; EZ U8.7-2 P109:7-8, P110:6-15, P111:3-9, P112:1-5, P112:26-52 merged with EZ U8.7-6 P253:1-9 and P250:1-7, one telling; JS U8.7-2 P107:4-10 with 59:24; JS U8.7-6 P248:2, P248:6-8 in Urdu, no book or author named; points U8.7.70, U8.7.68, U8.7.69, U8.7.71). [NEW: U8.7.70 (Teacher Copy), U8.7.68. PART: U8.7.69 recall B7 S11 "EZ: His creating is bound to no prior material, unlike human making that stalls without it" then new: the name Sani' against Khaliq and the carpenter, EZ U8.7-6 P250:1-4, U8.7-2 P112:48-52. PART: U8.7.71 recall B3 S09 "the Arafa words: no maker's making is like His making" then new: Fatir (35:1) and Ali's oath read as names of creating, EZ U8.7-6 P253:5-11]
  4. Which names tell that the world had a beginning? «یا مبدئ یا معید»: things were not and He brought them to be, so the kinds do not run back without a start; Mubdi' begins, Badi' brings the wholly new without model (2:117) (AH U8.7-2 P094:2-5 first; EZ U8.7-2 P098:4-13, P099:2-5, written once from this clearer text, not from U8.7-6 P251:18-19; JS U8.7-2 P097:4, P097:13-18, Saduq's gloss in Urdu; point U8.7.18). ↺ B3 S05 · B7 S12Already taught: U8.7.18 recall B3 S05 "the proof from origination" and B7 S12 "He made it the first time without a model"New here: the names Mubdi' and Badi' and AH drawing the world's beginning from «یا مبدئ», AH P094:2-5, EZ P098:5-13
  5. What do al-Malik al-Quddus, al-Mu'min and al-Muhaymin say? A King who is holy in His kingship, as no king, head or father is; holy even in His holiness; quddus stronger than subbuh; He grants security and does not receive it, and keeps watch over all; the believer is called mu'min in another sense, because he grants a security that God honours (59:23; AH U8.7-2 P084:3-15 first; JS P085:4-9; EZ P086:14-22, P087:15-19; EZ P079:6-15, P080:5-8; JS P078:2-5; MM P077:2; points U8.7.16, U8.7.14). ↺ B5 S02 · B5 S04Already taught: U8.7.16 recall B5 S02 "subbuh quddus is the intensive: holy without end" (B5 S04 too)New here: al-Malik with al-Quddus, AH's "holy even in His holiness", quddus stronger than subbuh, AH P084:3-15, EZ P086:14-22, P087:15-19. NEW: U8.7.14
  6. What are al-Jabbar, al-Mutakabbir and al-Sultan? Jabbar first mends the broken (the bone set by «یا جابر العظم الکسیر»), so the servant may say "we ruined it, You make it up", and only then breaks the tyrant; grandeur is His cloak; and the Sultan's grandeur is why He gives His will through intermediaries and does not deal directly with every low thing, though He is nearest to us (JS U8.7-2 P078:7-15 once; EZ U8.7-3 P143:8-12, P143:48-50, P150:8-20 cut to three lines; EZ U8.7-6 P254:1-16, P255:4-11; label line from EZ U8.7-2 P092:2-7 and JS P089:5-8; AH U8.7-5 P236:5-18, P236:24-26 grandeur line only; points U8.7.30, U8.7.72, U8.7.17, U8.7.58). [NEW: U8.7.30, U8.7.72. SAME: U8.7.17 recall only (below). PART: U8.7.58 recall B1 S09 "God does not by His Essence deal with each person without intermediary, so His commands come through a prophet" then new: the name Sultan and the king who does not sit chatting with the street sweeper, AH P236:7-18]
  7. How is His might? al-'Aziz is the victor never overcome, beyond reach; and He was Mighty when there was no might, because He was before His might (37:180); so «یا عزیزا لا یرام»; and al-'Ali: nothing is above Him (EZ U8.7-3 P155:13-45, P156:24-47; JS P153:1-23; JS U8.7-7 P275:1-10, two blocks merged; JS P273:5-11 one line, Teacher Copy; points U8.7.31, U8.7.86, U8.7.84). ↺ B7 S05Already taught: U8.7.31 recall B7 S05 "'izza is the state that cannot be overcome, His alone; Mighty and Wise (57:1)"New here: al-'Aziz explained as a name (beyond reach, never overcome) and 'Aziz with Hakim, EZ P155:24-P156:47, JS P153, P154:3-4. PART: U8.7.86 recall B7 S01 "His attributes are His before their objects" (B7 S05: might is His alone) then new: al-Baqir's "Mighty when there was no might" with 37:180 and "there was no time", JS P275:1-10. PART (verdict changed, below): U8.7.84 recall B5 S07 "Nahj sermon 49: He outran in height, nothing higher" then new: the name al-'Ali and «یا علی یا اعلی», JS P273:7-11, Teacher Copy
  8. Why does the Quran pair Qawi with another name, and what is Hakim? So that no one hears power without choice: the top row of the names is Knowledge and Power, and Qadir does what He wills (8:52, 22:40 endings inline, 22:18 inline); and Hakim is knowledge with firmness: the Knower, and the one whose works are firm and kept from all corruption (EZ U8.7-8 P295:1-16, P295:18-21; JS U8.7-3 P158:2-7, P159:5-10, Saduq's gloss in Urdu; EZ P161:17-20 one line; points U8.7.101, U8.7.32). ↺ B7 S11Already taught: U8.7.101 recall B7 S11 "EZ: power is dominion over doing and leaving, joined with choice"New here: the Quran pairing Qawi with a second name so that no power without choice is heard, EZ P295:4-11. PART: U8.7.32 recall B7 S21 "His wisdom comes from knowledge and power; the Wise loves firm work (EZ)" then new: the two lexical senses of al-Hakim, JS P158:2-7, P159:5-10 After step 8, Teacher Copy only: EZ on «شدید القوی» (53:5) as a name of God (EZ U8.7-8 P296:3-P298:15; point U8.7.102). ↺ B6 S06Already taught: U8.7.102 recall B6 S06 "EZ sets two readings of 53:5 to 10: Jibril by the plain sense, or God, by «عبده»"New here: EZ's verdict for God and his second argument (Jibril brings the message and is not the teacher, 4:113), P297:5-7, P298:6-15

Next lesson picks up

«بندہ بگاڑ دے تو وہ کیسے درگزر کرتا ہے: اس کے ہاں رحمت پہلے ہے یا غضب؟» (How does He pass over what the servant has ruined: which comes first in Him, mercy or wrath?) (S12)

S12 وہ کریمانہ بخشتا ہے: اس کی رحمت اس کے غضب سے اور اس کا عفو اس کی سزا سے آگے ہے؛ وہ درجہ بہ درجہ درگزر کرتا، ڈھانپتا اور مٹاتا ہے، شرمندہ نہیں کرتا، جلد راضی ہوتا ہے اور برائی کو نیکی میں بدل دیتا ہے؛ مگر وہ حکیم بھی ہے، اس لیے اس کا کرم نہ گناہ کی چھوٹ ہے اور نہ ناامیدی کی کوئی جگہ ہے

ledger H12 · 3 new · 10 part-taught before · 3 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Which comes first in Him, mercy or wrath, pardon or punishment? Mercy and pardon: in the scroll the Najran party opened, God says «فأنا العزيز الحكيم الرحمن الرحيم أرحم وأترحم سبقت رحمتي غضبي وعفوي عقوبتي» (MM U8.7-5 P234:4-6, the Jami excerpt cut to this clause, the recording's garbled «وفاصل الأسباب» not printed; P234:2-3 the Najran setting in one line; P234:7-9 Adam's question on the prophets out, Ask; U8.7.56). Teacher Copy under the recall: EZ U8.5-2 P095:1-10 (15:49-50, forgiveness named before punishment; U8.5.19). ↺ B4 S02 · B7 S23Already taught: B4 S02 "MM's Seth scroll narration: God sends the messengers «بتوحيدي»" and B7 S23 step 2 "His mercy outran His wrath",New here: the clause «وعفوي عقوبتي», pardon outrunning punishment, read for the names of pardon, in MM P234:4-6 and MM's sentence after the block | U8.5.19 SAME: recall only
  2. How does He pass over a sin? In stages: safh (He turns His face as if He saw nothing), satr (a veil that can be lifted), ghafr (a covering stuck fast so the trace is gone), 'afw (wiping out), and beyond 'afw He writes the mistakes as right and gives marks for them: He turns evil deeds into good (EZ U8.7-3 P138:3-30, P139:55-71, the dictation paper told once; the Abu Hamza line in Jami's order; P138:31-33 people's rights one line; U8.7.27). [core repeat kept in full: B7 S25 steps 1 and 4 teach maghfira as covering, 'afw as wiping, and 25:70 (Ask)]
  3. What is noble pardon? He forgives without shaming, waves the apology away and changes the subject; He keeps no one waiting, and rejoices at the return more than a traveller who finds his lost mount (JS U8.7-7 P276:1-21 as the one telling, «کریم العفو», «خیر الغافرین», «حسن التجاوز»; JS U8.7-3 P127:1-12, P128:12-41, P131:8-11, P132:18-38 merged into it, not retold; U8.7.27). Teacher Copy under the recall: the angels who pitied man and God's answer (JS P276:23-P277:19; U8.7.87). [core repeat kept in full: B7 S25 step 3 "He pardons generously without shaming and rejoices at the return" (Ask) | U8.7.87 SAME: recall only]
  4. What has mastery to do with pardon? He is «سید السادات», Master of masters, and overlooking a fault is the mark of a master: people call a man a master when he passes over his servant's disobedience (AH U8.7-5 P238:1-2, P239:1-2; U8.7.60). Then the servant comes to such a Master as a sinner at His door (AH P241:1-12, one line); the poor man who sells his cloak rather than send ten visitors away empty is recalled in one line (P241:13-34). Teacher Copy, fine layer: Imam Husayn's munajat at Khadija's grave and the reply from the unseen (AH P241:38-52; U8.7.61; Ask). ↺ B1 S06Already taught: B1 S06 "AH: mastery is known by the open hand, and God is the Master of masters" and B1 S06 "AH: if a mean man would not turn back those at his door, how much more the Generous",New here: the Jawshan name Sayyid al-Sadat and overlooking faults as the mark of mastery, in AH P238:1-2, P239:1-2; the munajat (P241:38-52) as the new part of U8.7.61, TC only
  5. How fast is He pleased? He is «سریع الرضا»: our wrongs came from ignorance, not from defiance, and the moment we say in earnest "we did not understand, we have come", He is pleased, before we finish thinking it (JS U8.7-7 P278:1-13, P279:1-7 once; JS U8.7-3 P128:39-41 not retold; U8.7.88). Teacher Copy: Imam al-'Askari's saying, paraphrase only (P277:20-26, Ask). ↺ B7 S25Already taught: B7 S25 steps 2 and 3 "sincere regret is repentance and is forgiven at once; He rejoices at the return",New here: the name sari' al-rida itself, in JS P278:10-13, P279:5-7
  6. What do Ghaffar, Ghafur, Rahim and Tawwab add? Ghaffar for many sins, Ghafur for great ones: 5:34 says Ghafur of the one who warred on God and repented before he was caught; Rahim after Ghafur, because He does not only pardon, He also gives; «وإني لغفار لمن تاب وآمن وعمل صالحا ثم اهتدى» with its conditions; Tawwab, the mother who runs ten steps when the child takes two (JS U8.7-3 P177:5-9, P177:47-56, P179:1-12, P181:17-P182:4, P178:1-7; EZ P185:27-28, P186:14-20; JS P183:3-6; 15:49 and 20:82 once each; the Kaaba reading of 20:82, JS U8.7-8 P285:23-27, one line without the student (Ask); U8.7.36, U8.7.92). ↺ B7 S25 · B7 S23Already taught: B7 S25 step 1 "maghfira covers the sin, 'afw wipes it" and B7 S23 step 2 (15:49),New here: Ghaffar and Ghafur told apart, Rahim after Ghafur, Tawwab as a name, in JS P177:50-56, P182:1-4, EZ P186:14-19, JS P183:3-6 (U8.7.36) | NEW: the Kaaba reading of 20:82 (U8.7.92)
  7. Why does He not hasten, and is His giving a reproach? Halim: «سبحان من هو رحيم لا يعجل»; He gives respite so the sinner may return, and He does not hasten because nothing can slip from His hand. Mannan: mann is giving a blessing, not reproaching (49:17's last clause), and the greatest gift is that He rewards by grace, not by bargain, while He punishes by justice. Rabb al-'afw: He is the owner of pardon, so none may ask why He pardoned, and the Eid qunut joins His grandeur with pardon (EZ U8.7-3 P164:1-25 with the block; JS P163:3-21, Saduq's gloss in Urdu; EZ P166:1-62 cut, JS P165:1-6 with «إنك أنت المنان بالجسيم» inline; JS P167:5-P169:29 with the sa'y du'a in Jami's wording; EZ P173:1-10 one inline line in Jami's order; U8.7.33, U8.7.34, U8.7.35). Teacher Copy, fine layer: al-Hannan, the name the Hannana pillar explains, «تحننوا» for the transcript's «تهننوا» (EZ P142:1-7; U8.7.29; Ask). ↺ B7 S26Already taught: B7 S26 step 5 "He does not hasten punishment and gives the sinner time",New here: the point as the meaning of the name al-Halim, in EZ P164:18-25, JS P163:3-8 (U8.7.33) | PART: recall B7 S04 step 7 "why God, who forbids reproach, speaks of His favour", then new: al-Mannan the giver, not the reproacher, from the root of mann, in EZ P166:7-24, JS P165:1-6 (U8.7.34) | NEW: Rabb al-'afw, rabb as owner, and the Eid qunut (U8.7.35) | PART: recall B1 S07 "AH: the palm trunk that wept for the Prophet", then new: EZ reads it for the name al-Hannan, P142:5-7, TC only (U8.7.29)
  8. Then may the servant sin and say "He is Karim", or despair? Neither. 82:6: His generosity must not lure one into sin, for the Karim is also Hakim and does not treat good and bad alike (recall). And never despair: when we say «یا ارحم الراحمین» it means no mercy reaches His, «أنت أرحم من كل رحيم»; He wrongs no one an atom's weight and repays good with increase; He is 'Afuw, Karim, Jawad, Muhsin, His beauty has a thousand names, and He does not leave the fasting servant unrewarded (JS U8.7-3 P141:1-7, P165:7-8; JS U8.7-8 P286:2-8 with 4:40 (Ask); AH U8.7-5 P245:1 one line; the Eid-night report P245:2 paraphrase only, Ask; U8.7.89, U8.7.28, U8.7.93, U8.7.65). Teacher Copy under the recall: JS P280:4-P281:11. Imports for this step, TC only: U13.19/4, U13.19/3, U13.19/14, U13.20/3 (below). One paragraph that hands on to S13. [U8.7.89 SAME: recall only | PART: recall B7 S23 step 7 "do not despair of His mercy (39:53)", then new: Arham al-Rahimin read as a name, in JS P141:6-7, P165:7-8 (U8.7.28) | PART: recall B7 S21 step 2 "He can wrong and does not (10:44)" and B7 S23 step 5 "reward is by His grace", then new: 4:40 itself, in JS P286:2-6 (U8.7.93) | NEW: AH's thousand names of beauty assure the faster's reward (U8.7.65)]

Imports 16
  • Kept U13.19/4 (MM MMB_11_S08 ¶352; AH QR-S04 ¶97-111), TC under step 8. Claude's judgement Adam's word in the same Seth scroll as step 1, «حق لمن عرفك بنعمتك أن لا يعصيك بها، ولمن علم سعة رحمتك ومغفرتك أن لا ييأس منها», states both halves of step 8 (no licence, no despair), and AH's "despair of His mercy is unforgivable; we have come to Your carpet, ya Arham al-Rahimin" is the servant's side of Arham al-Rahimin. Sound fit.
  • Kept U13.19/3 (MM MMB_09_S07 ¶320, the Kumayl counsel), TC under step 8. Claude's judgement "they make your opinion of God good so that you hope in Him, are deceived and disobey Him" names the very error step 8 forbids, taking His generosity as a licence. Sound fit; the Arabic is not yet checked against Jami.
  • Kept U13.19/14 (MM MMB_11_S17 ¶763), TC under step 8, the Rajab-fasting tail (¶7-11 of the passage) out. Claude's judgement "his hope in God rescued him from the brink of Hell; his good opinion of God stilled his trembling on the Bridge" shows hope in the Most Merciful as itself a door of His rescue, so the servant must never despair. Fair fit (a Ma'ad scene used for one line).
  • Kept U13.19/7 (AH SE-S02 ¶66-69, QR-S04 ¶38), TC under step 5, the confession to the audience (passage lines 20 to 39) out. Claude's judgement the knower who slips turns back at once in tears, calling «تو کریمی، تو رحیمی، تو عفوی، تو غفوری، تو آقایی», and still stays between fear and hope; He gives without our desert, so we hope. This is the servant's side of sari' al-rida and Sayyid al-Sadat. Sound fit.
  • Kept U13.20/3, AH half only (QR-S04 ¶76-82), TC under step 8; MM's half (MMB_05_S05 ¶216, the suspended ra'y and the prophets' fear, Z2) not taken. Claude's judgement "His giving has no cause but His grace and His taking no measure but His justice; the One who gave can take by morning" supports "Karim and also Hakim": His generosity is no licence. Fair fit only; AH's coarse examples (usury, the gaze) stay out of the Handout.

  • Dropped U13.20/1: no sound framing; reading the Quran with humility and fear.
  • Dropped U13.20/2: no sound framing; night prayer in fear and hope, and MM's passage is the Friday reward in Paradise.
  • Dropped U13.20/4: no sound framing; bada as the key to du'a (Z2), not a step of how He pardons.
  • Dropped U13.20/5: adds a voice only; the balance of hope in His grace and fear of His justice is already carried by U13.19/7 and U13.19/4, and MM's half is bada (Z2).

Next lesson picks up

بندہ کریم کے حضور کیا لے کر جائے، اور کن ناموں سے اس سے بخشش مانگے؟ (What does the servant bring before the Generous, and by which names does he ask Him for pardon?) (S13)

S13 کریم کے حضور بندہ خالی ہاتھ آتا ہے اور اسی کے سکھائے ہوئے ناموں سے بخشش مانگتا ہے: وہ خود معافی کی دعوت دیتا ہے، عرش کے خزانوں سے اپنے نام بھیجتا ہے اور پناہ مانگنے والے کو رد نہیں کرتا

ledger H13 · 6 new · 1 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 6
  1. What does a servant bring to the Generous? Nothing, and that is the right way to come: the verse said to have been written on Salman's shroud, «وفدت على الكريم بغير زاد»; its first half makes him fear (no good deeds, no sound heart), its second half makes him hope (to carry provision to the Generous is the ugliest thing); so he comes in his helplessness and calls «یا کریم». (JS U8.7-8 P283:2-18, P284:2, P284:7-9; the verse as «منسوب», Jami's «أقبح»; U8.7.91; Teacher Copy: kept import U13.14/21, AH.) [U8.7.91 NEW]
  2. How does He Himself invite forgiveness? On the Day, when the wronged and the wrongdoers stand pressed together, His caller says: «أنا الوهاب إن أحببتم أن تواهبوا فتواهبوا», and a silver palace of Firdaws is shown «لكل من عفا عن مؤمن». The One who asks us to forgive one another is the One we ask to forgive us. (MM U8.7-5 P235:1-3, P235:9-13 cut to the two lines, the rest in one prose line from P235:4-8; P235 closing line; U8.7.57.) [U8.7.57 NEW]
  3. Which names did Jibril bring from the treasures of the Throne? He came down laughing with a gift, «كلمات من كنوز العرش»: «يا من أظهر الجميل وستر القبيح، يا من لم يؤاخذ بالجريرة ولم يهتك الستر، يا عظيم العفو، يا حسن التجاوز، يا واسع المغفرة ... يا مبتدئا بالنعم قبل استحقاقها». He brings the good to light however the servant hides it, covers the ugly again and again unless the servant himself makes a show of it, does not seize at once, and pardons without shaming; and He gave before anything was deserved. Three or four names glossed in the Handout, the rest in the Teacher Copy. (JS U8.7-3 P188:11-23, P187:19-33 (verified block, Miras-e Hadith 15:251), P189:1-12, P189:54-55, P189:75-79; U8.7-8 P188:15-22, P288:4-8, P288:11, P289:12-20 merged into that one telling; AH RR8-048 under the last line, recalled; U8.7.37, .95.) [U8.7.37 core repeat kept in full: SAME B7 S25 "the dua's first line read as His covering of our sins, Sattar al-'uyub", kept because the Jibril names are this lesson's core step (Ask 2) | U8.7.95 NEW | RR8-048 SAME: recall B1 S16 only]
  4. Why is the reward of these words so great? Jibril himself says knowledge stops short of it: «هيهات هيهات انقطع العلم...». It is not strange: the servant describes God as God loves to be described and as He taught His Prophet, with belief and humility; nothing is hard for His power and His generosity has no like. (JS U8.7-3 P187:34-39 (verified); U8.7-8 P287:1-15, P289:5-10; the poet's dream U8.7-7 P282 Teacher Copy only if Ali keeps it; U8.7.94, .90.) [U8.7.94 NEW | U8.7.90 NEW (Teacher Copy only, recommended drop: Ask 10)]
  5. Where does the servant take refuge? In His noble Face, His might that none can reach and His power from which nothing escapes; the Generous does not turn away one who seeks His refuge. (JS U8.7-8 P290:1-25 as one block in Jami wording, al-Kafi 3:334; U8.7.96.) [U8.7.96 NEW]
  6. With what words does he ask? With the Imam's munajat, «إلهي ما أنا بأول من عصاك فتبت عليه» (recalled), and in the night prayer's order: seventy times «أستغفر الله ربي وأتوب إليه» to cover the sin, then three hundred times «العفو» to cleanse it, and only then the request for good. (JS U8.7-8 P291:1-22, recalled; EZ P292:10-11, P292:14-15, P292:19-20 for the new order; U8.7.97, .98; Teacher Copy: kept import U13.14/21, AH's istighfar formula.) [U8.7.97 SAME: recall B7 S25 only | U8.7.98 PART: recall B7 S25 "maghfira covers the sin, 'afw wipes it out", then new: the night prayer's seventy istighfar and three hundred «العفو», in EZ P292:10-11, P292:14-15, P292:19-20]

Imports 18
  • Kept U13.21/1 (MM MMB_11_S23 ¶1048, al-Ikhtisas: "My servant, were you not ashamed, did you not watch Me?" "My Master, I did wrong, do not disgrace me, the creatures are looking." "By My might, O wrongdoer, I will not disgrace you today": the sins stay hidden between him and God and the good deeds are shown to the creatures; "what you did well is thanked, what you did ill is forgiven"). Teacher Copy only, beside step 3. Claude's judgement this narration shows on the Day what the Jibril du'a's first lines name, the One who shows the beautiful and covers the ugly and does not tear the veil, so it supports step 3's reading of «أظهر الجميل وستر القبيح» and «لم يهتك الستر». Only MM's narration is taken; JS's repentance out of shame (PUB_0076 ¶42, PUB_0161 ¶56-58) is not. The text is not in the Bab 8 Jami check (a U13 text): verify before print.
  • Kept U13.14/21, only its closing part (AH AZ-S21 ¶207-211; SE-S29 ¶96-98; ZH-S34 ¶148-150: "from the beggar comes only begging, from God only Godhood; we have done our begging, now it rests on Your mastery"; the istighfar formula «أستغفر الله الذي لا إله إلا هو الرحمن الرحيم ذو الجلال والإكرام بديع السماوات والأرض وأتوب إليه», "do not be ashamed before God to repent"). Teacher Copy only, beside steps 1 and 6. Claude's judgement AH's beggar line says what step 1 says, that the servant brings nothing to the Generous but his need and the Generous does not send him away empty; and his formula is a set istighfar made of God's names, which fits step 6 (with what words does he ask). Overlap: B3 S08 already has AH's "our begging shows His Godhood" (RR-H08/P020:6-7), B7 S25 recalls the "pen of pardon" plea (RR1-055) and B4 S09 the confession of His sovereignty (RR4-019); so keep only the beggar line and the formula. Not taken from this row: the wudu, hujub, Sayfi Saghir and the lecturer's teacher's prayer (P086 to P090).
  • Dropped U13.21/2 (Fudayl and 57:16): no sound framing: a repentance story with no name of God asked by, and Fudayl is already taught in B1 S13 (recalled in B7 S25, RR2-021).

  • Dropped U13.14/2 (self-disclosures in His speech before dawn): no sound framing: night intimacy, no pardon step.

  • Dropped U13.14/9 (remembrance is a wijdani reminder, 13:28; his night near Bojnurd): no sound framing: dhikr, U8.11 ground.

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «وفدت على الكريم بغير زاد ... وحمل الزاد أقبح كل شيء إذا كان الوفود على الكريم» (JS U8.7-8 P283:4, P283:17): POEM, late sources only (Zad al-Ma'ad 1:575; al-Anwar al-Nu'maniyya 4:164; La'ali al-Akhbar 4:265); print as a verse «منسوب», «أقبح» not «أغبه».
  2. «أنا الوهاب إن أحببتم أن تواهبوا فتواهبوا» and «هذا لكل من عفا عن مؤمن» (MM U8.7-5 P235): FOUND, al-Kafi 8 (Rawda) via Mir'at al-'uqul 25:256; the excerpt starts mid-phrase and carries «يقول كم]»: fix.
  3. «يا من أظهر الجميل وستر القبيح، يا من لم يؤاخذ بالجريرة ولم يهتك الستر، يا عظيم العفو، يا حسن التجاوز، يا واسع المغفرة ... يا مبتدئا بالنعم قبل استحقاقها» (JS U8.7-3 P187:19-33): FOUND, Miras-e Hadith 15:251, Saduq's Tawhid (a non-Shi'i chain), Iqbal, al-Balad al-Amin 547; print «بالجريرة», «مبتدئا», «منتهى كل شكوى»; the eight H8 one-line blocks give way to this one block.
  4. «هيهات هيهات انقطع العلم، لو اجتمع ملائكة سبع سماوات وسبع أرضين...» (JS U8.7-3 P187:38-39): FOUND, Miras-e Hadith 15:246; Saduq's Tawhid.
  5. «أعوذ بوجهك الكريم وعزتك التي لا ترام وقدرتك التي لا يمتنع منها شيء من شر الدنيا والآخرة وشر الأوجاع كلها» (JS U8.7-8 P290): FOUND-OTHER-SPEAKER, al-Kafi 3:334, Abu al-Hasan in Jami, not al-Sadiq; restore «كلها».
  6. «إلهي ما أنا بأول من عصاك فتبت عليه» (JS U8.7-8 P291:13, recalled): FOUND-OTHER-WORDING, Munajat al-Ta'ibin (Zad al-Ma'ad 1:407; Bihar 91:142-143), not al-Mustaghfirin; print «عصاك ... عليه».
  7. «أستغفر الله ربي وأتوب إليه» seventy times and «العفو» three hundred times (EZ U8.7-8 P292:10-15): an order of the night prayer, not in the Jami check; the source of the order is to be given (diag).
Next lesson picks up

اللہ کے ناموں میں جمال بھی ہے اور جلال بھی: وہ ایک ہی وقت میں دیتا بھی ہے اور لیتا بھی، تو یہ دونوں کیسے ایک ہیں اور بندے کا دل ان دونوں کے سامنے کیا کرے؟ (His names hold beauty and majesty: He gives and takes at once; how are the two one, and what should the servant's heart do before each?) (S14)

S14 خدا اپنے جمال کے ناموں سے دیتا اور اپنے جلال کے ناموں سے لیتا ہے؛ یہ دونوں ایک دوسرے میں ہیں، اس کا قہر بھی مہر ہے، اور اس کا ہر جلوہ بندے سے اپنا خاص نیاز مانگتا ہے

ledger · 7 new · 9 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 9
  1. Where does bringing a heart to God begin? With God Himself: His attributes of majesty, so that people fear, and of beauty, so that they love; and the Quran's stories carry both: majesty and wrath in Lut's people overturned and Pharaoh's drowned, beauty, ihsan and ihsan in Yusuf drawn from the well and kept safe in the closed house (AH U8.7-7 P262:1-2 with 96:1-5 cut to «اقرأ باسم ربك ... وربك الأكرم»; AH P263:1-6, 12:53 one line (Ask); points U8.7.79, U8.7.80). ↺ B1 S09recall B1 S09 "AH: first God, His attributes of majesty so people fear and of beauty so they love" (U8.7.79; verdict changed from PART: B1 S09 step 6 prints this very AH passage, MV-S07 ¶12 with 96:1-5) | PART: recall B1 S09 "MM: the Quran's aim is to show His majesty and beauty" then new: the stories carry it, Lut and Pharaoh for majesty, Yusuf for beauty, in AH's P263:2-6 (U8.7.80)
  2. Can His many names be sorted? Into two classes: names of beauty by which He gives (Muhsin, Mujmil, Mun'im, Basit, Muhyi) and names of majesty by which He takes back (Qabid, Mumit, Ghalib, Qahhar); under each name lies a particular knowledge; He holds all things between the two hands of His beauty and majesty and turns them over every moment, «کل یوم هو فی شأن»; He wiped out Ad and Thamud, drowned Pharaoh, raised nations to rule and brought them down; «تؤتی الملک ... وتنزع الملک ... تعز ... تذل» read by the names: by al-'Aziz He gives honour, by al-Qahhar He takes it, by al-Muhyi He gives being, by al-Mumit He takes it. EZ: the names of wrath (Mutakabbir, Jabbar, Mubir al-zalimin) act by justice in punishment and by grace in reward (AH U8.7-2 P119:1-4, P116:1-6, P117:2-5, P118:2-7; AH U8.7-5 P237:35-42, P237:1-11, P237:43-46; AH U8.7-6 P256:1-8, P256:10; AH U8.7-6 P124:2-4 = U8.7-2 P124:2-3, one home; 3:26 once; EZ U8.7-2 P120:5-20 with the Iftitah block; points U8.7.23, U8.7.59, U8.7.73, U8.7.74). [CORE REPEAT kept in full: B1 S06 (U8.7.23) | PART: recall B1 S06 "AH: He gives by His beauty and takes by His majesty, always at work" then new: the pairs Qabid and Basit, Muhyi and Mumit with 55:29, in P237:6-11, 35-43 (U8.7.59); Ad, Thamud and Pharaoh destroyed by the names of majesty, in P256:1-7 (U8.7.73) | PART: recall B7 S11 "AH: 3:26, He gives the kingdom and takes it away" then new: the verse read by the names, al-'Aziz gives honour, al-Qahhar takes it, in P124:2-4 (U8.7.74)]
  3. If God does not hesitate, what does «ما ترددت» mean? The two names at work: giving and taking look like the one who steps forward and back; their fullest work is in the believer, the fruit of the tree of this world; so the heart has two feasts every moment, one of passing away and one of remaining. Three readings, each denying ignorance or change in God (AH U8.7-2 P123:1, P123:4, P125:1-3; AH U8.7-6 P124:1, P124:5-7 = U8.7-2 P124, once, without the "great teacher" and Mulla Sadra's name; the hadith as one block; JS UNK_0009 ¶56, AQD_0053 ¶8-11 and EZ AHL_0559 ¶14 not gathered (Ask); point U8.7.25). ↺ B1 S06Already taught: B1 S06 "AH: in God taraddud is the succession of beauty and majesty, at its fullest in the believer" (for AH a full repeat)New here: JS's reading by supposition, EZ's reading against philosophy
  4. Why is the believer's heart turned so? It lies between two fingers of the Merciful, that is between the hand of beauty that gives life and the hand of majesty that takes it (God has no hands); the heart is called qalb for its taqallub: joy to grief, knowledge to forgetting; so we call Him «یا مقلب القلوب», and EZ: the Turner of hearts can turn back a child who has left religion, so never despair (AH U8.7-6 P257:1-7 with AH U8.7-2 P121:1-2, 6-10, same lecture AZ-S03; EZ U8.7-2 P122:8-11, the P122:58 block, P122:16-23, 32-34; points U8.7.75, U8.7.24). [NEW (U8.7.75) | PART: recall B1 S06 "AH: God turns hearts and states; the turning of our states is His self-introduction" then new: the turning as the alternation of beauty and majesty (P121:1-2, 9-10) and EZ's hope for the child who left religion (P122:16-34) (U8.7.24)]
  5. Are beauty and majesty two things? No: His beauty is in His majesty and His majesty in His beauty; beauty intensified turns to majesty, as when the women saw Yusuf and cut their hands (12:31), and majesty seen with care is beauty; when He gives much, the servant is lost in wonder (AH U8.7-6 P258:9-13, P259:1-5, P260:1-6 short; 108:1 one inline line (Ask); RR8-072 recalled; RR8-083 Teacher Copy; point U8.7.77). ↺ B1 S06Already taught: B1 S06 "AH: in His majesty is beauty and in His beauty majesty"New here: beauty intensified becomes majesty, the women who saw Yusuf, in P258:12-13, P259:1-5, P260:2-6
  6. Then what is His wrath and punishment? The ground of His being is generosity and wrath is only dust upon it, «یا من سبقت رحمته غضبه»; His majesty is in His beauty because He wants to cleanse: the copper mixed in gold goes to the furnace, and the fire's wrath is a mother's love for the gold; so 'adhab is from 'adhb (AH U8.7-6 P261:1-4; RR8-072 ¶63 recalled; point U8.7.78). ↺ B1 S06Already taught: B1 S06 "AH: even His taking is grace; His mercy outran His wrath"New here: the furnace's love for the gold and 'adhab from 'adhb, in P261:1-4
  7. What does each grace ask of the servant? A need fitted to it: His manifestations are as many as His names; the manifestations of beauty as many as the names of love (Latif, Ra'uf, Shafiq, Muhsin, Mujmil), of majesty as many as the names of wrath; one speaks to a friend, a teacher and a king in three ways; each favour asks its thanks, and His thousand and one graces ask for love, intimacy and awe in turn (AH U8.7-7 P264:1-6, P264:17-29, P265:1-6, P266:1-10 short; RR8-066 recalled; point U8.7.81). [NEW (U8.7.81) | SAME: RR8-066 recalled only]
  8. Which names of beauty comfort the servant? Ra'uf, mercy at its intensity, with its own sense beyond Rahman and Rahim; Shafiq and Rafiq of Du'a al-Mashlul (Teacher Copy); the Anis of the lonely in the dark and the Neighbour of one who has no neighbour; God is beautiful and loves beauty (Jamil); He is high, yet near: raise Him high, but not so high that you go far from Him (JS U8.7-2 P126:3-8; AH U8.7-2 P100:1-7 and EZ P101:17-22 Teacher Copy; AH U8.7-5 P242:1-15; RR8-047 recalled; EZ U8.7-8 P293:5 re-aimed (Ask); EZ P294:1-9, 16-17, the tasbih one line (Ask); points U8.7.26, U8.7.19, U8.7.62, U8.7.99, U8.7.100). [NEW (U8.7.26, U8.7.19 Teacher Copy, U8.7.62, U8.7.99) | PART: recall B5 S07 "He is near in His farness and far in His nearness (EZ among the holders)" then new: the tasbih and EZ's advice to raise Him high without going far, in P294:1-9 (U8.7.100) | SAME: RR8-047 recalled only]
  9. (optional, Ask) What becomes of one who realises a name? Realised in al-Salam, Ibrahim took the benefit of water from the fire (21:69); realised in al-Mu'min, a safety that the world's collapse does not shake; realised in Rafiq, a fellowship with all things that he does not explain (AH U8.7-5 P243:1-7; point U8.7.63). [NEW, Teacher Copy only]

Who carries it

AH carries steps 1 to 7 and 9 alone (BJ-S19, AZ-S03, SE-S17, ZH-S03, QR-S02, MV-S07, MV-S08, SE-S27, ML-S10); EZ adds at step 2 (the names of wrath, justice and grace), step 4 (the child turned back) and step 8 (Jamil, high yet near; rifq as practice in the Teacher Copy); JS adds Ra'uf (step 8) and a reading of taraddud (step 3, not gathered). MM is absent: his reading of the heart's turning by God's will over the lights is outside Bab 8 (atlas note on U8.7.24); his "Quran shows majesty and beauty" is only the B1 S09 recall at step 1. The lesson's thesis itself is a core repeat of B1 S06 (Ask).

Texts and Jami results

«ما تردّدت في شيء أنا فاعله كتردّدي في قبض روح عبدي المؤمن» (hadith qudsi; Jami: Majma' al-Bahrayn 3:48 «وفي الحديث القدسي»; Ikhtiyar al-Misbah 1:106-107; Miras-e Hadith 2:286; one home across U8.7-2 and U8.7-6); 3:26 «قل اللهم مالك الملك تؤتي الملك من تشاء وتنزع الملك ممن تشاء وتعز من تشاء وتذل من تشاء» (once); 12:31 «فلما رأينه أكبرنه وقطعن أيديهن وقلن حاش لله ما هذا بشرا»; «يا من سبقت رحمته غضبه» (Jami: Jawshan Kabir section 19, al-Balad al-Amin 546, Bihar 91:386; Iqbal 2); «قلب المؤمن بين إصبعين من أصابع الرحمن» (no Arabic in the lesson; Jami: late and Sufi-carried works only, 'Awali al-La'ali 4:99, Mir'at 10:394, Amali al-Murtada 1:318-324 treats it as a non-Shi'i report); «يا مقلب القلوب والأبصار ...» (Jami: Zad al-Ma'ad 1:328 only, «في غير الكتب المشهورة», no Imam named); «إن الله جميل يحب الجمال» (Jami: Kanz al-Daqa'iq 14:324, Tafsir al-Safi 2:189, Imam Hasan dressing for prayer; no «و» before «يحب»). Also: «يا شفيق يا رفيق فكني من حلق المضيق» and «يا جار من لا جار له» FOUND in Du'a al-Mashlul (al-Misbah 1:262-263; Bihar 92:400-401); «يا أنيس المستوحشين في الظلم» FOUND-OTHER-WORDING, Jami «يا مونس المستوحشين في الظلم» (Jamal al-Usbu' 1:410-411); the tasbih FOUND-OTHER-SPEAKER, the Prophet's du'a (Muhaj al-Da'awat 1:109-110), Jami «من ماجد ما أرأفه» for the garbled «من مجيد ما أرفه».

Next lesson picks up

کچھ سورتیں جدا جدا حروف سے کیوں شروع ہوتی ہیں، اور ان حروف میں اللہ کے ناموں کی کیا بات ہے؟ (Why do some suras open with separate letters, and what do they hold of God's names?) (S15)

6. The letters and the Greatest Name S15 to S16

S15 قرآن کے حروفِ مقطعات اللہ کے نام بھی ہیں اور رمز بھی؛ ان کی کنجی معصومین علیہم السلام کے پاس ہے، اور ایک حرف «ہ» بتاتا ہے کہ وہ ثابت ذات حواس سے غائب ہے

ledger · 10 new · 12 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why do some suras open with separate letters? God Himself chose exactly these letters; the Prophet only recites them as given; the Quran opens with «الم»، «ق»، «حم», never with «ابج» or a lone «ل» or «ج», so they hold secrets and keep a reckoning (AH P024:1-8; RR8-078, RR8-079). ↺ B1 S12recall B1 S12 "God chose these letters, the Prophet only recites, there are secrets in them" (RR8-078, RR8-079); AH P024:1-8 (Qaf, not «ل و القرآن») is the same illustration and sits under the recall
  2. What are they? Names of God or pointers to His names, and also codes between God, the Prophet and the Imams; the two do not conflict, as one sign can be a name and a code at once (JS P003:1-9, P002:12-20 with his «الف» = ten matters example; AH P001:16-17). ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the letters are cipher keys to the Quran's inner (AH)"New here: they are also names of God, and names and codes do not conflict (U8.8.1)
  3. How do we know they are names? The Imams call God by them in their du'as: «يا كهيعص», «يا حم عسق» (AH P001:7-13, JS P002:20-21); and al-Sadiq opened most of them as names, «الم: أنا الله الملك», while Taha and Yasin are names of the Prophet (AH P027:2-4, trimmed). Teacher Copy: joining «الر»، «حم»، «ن» gives «الرحمن» (AH P026:1-5); Qaf read as al-Qayyum (AH P004:11-13) or as the qaf names, Qawi and Qahhar (JS P005:4-5, P006:6-9), one line each beside the hadith's mountain. ↺ Bab 1 to 7Already taught: new part of U8.8.1, the letters called upon in du'a; NEW: U8.8.10, U8.8.9 (TC), U8.8.2 (TC)
  4. Who can read them? Only those who hold the keys. They are the Quran's quintessence («إن لكل كتاب صفوة وصفوة القرآن حروف التهجي») and hold «علماً جماً»; every disjointed letter is from the letters of the Greatest Name, which the Prophet and the Imam compose and call by and are answered; which letter stands for which name only the Imams know, and nothing is to be said past the narrations (AH P024:9-13, P025:2, P026:6-8, P001:18-19). Greatest Name detail left to S16. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "al-Baqir: great knowledge in the disjointed letters, keys held by the wasi while we are camel herders" (RR8-080)New here: they are the Quran's quintessence and the Greatest Name is composed from them (U8.8.7); NEW: U8.8.8
  5. Is every letter a name? Amir al-Mu'minin to the Jew: «ما من حرف إلا وهو اسم من أسماء الله», with one example, «أما الألف فالله الذي لا إله إلا هو الحي القيوم»; a letter points to a name or to a reality, as the abjad told by the infant Jesus shows (one line) (JS P008:4-15, P011:12-13, P012:2-5 in one line, P014:4). ↺ B7 S03Already taught: B7 S03 "the letters and abjad hadiths of al-Tawhid read for the attributes (JS), Teacher Copy"New here: every letter is a name of God (JS P008:13-15, P011:12-13) (U8.8.3)
  6. Then may we pursue a science of letters? It is noble and its rules came from the Imams (Ali's jafr and the Jami'a; the derivation of «الله» and «الصمد» in the narrations), but no one now holds its law of extraction: those who claim it lie, misunderstand or deceive themselves; it comes only by the grace of wilaya and through the narrations (AH P007:1, P007:10-16, P007:17-28, P007:35-36). So any time for the reappearance drawn by geomancy, even with the circle of letters, or by jafr is certainly false (RR8-076). Teacher Copy: every letter has a limit, each hamza (of «الله», of «الصمد») its own allusion, and the question of the article of al-Samad (AH MV-S04 ¶40-43), as U8.5 decides. ↺ B7 S08Already taught: new AH part of U8.8.3 (the noble science, no law of extraction now); PART: recall B7 S08 "no time has been set for the reappearance, and jafr is rejected for it"New here: geomancy with the circle of letters is certainly false, AH's own witness (RR8-076); NEW: U8.8.29 (TC); PART: U8.8.30 (TC)
  7. One letter as an example: the ha of Huwa. The root of the name Huwa is the ha; it is round, with no part, angle or end, a sign of the fixed Essence with no first or last; and «هو» is the pronoun of the far and absent: when they said «هذه آلهتنا», the answer came «قل هو الله أحد», He is «هو», not «هذا» (AH P043:1-2, P043:8-14, P045:1-10, P045:44-48, P047:1). Teacher Copy: the breath, in «ہا» and out «ہو» (P044); the ha's number five, multiplied by itself, ends in five (MV-S09 ¶27-31); the one-eyed ha that becomes two-eyed (U8.7-6 AH P258:1-11); the same ha in «الله» (P045:49 to P046:2), one line or none as U8.5 decides. ↺ B4 S06 · B6 S01Already taught: B4 S06 "the Greatest Name narration, la ilaha illa Huwa" and B6 S01 "Huwa names the One absent from sight (al-Baqir on ha and waw)"New here: the Greatest Name located in the letter ha, its root and its round shape (U8.8.22); SAME: recall B4 S06 the circle (RR8-060); PART (TC): U8.8.23, U8.7.76; NEW: U8.8.24 (TC), U8.8.25
  8. What does this ha say of all things? «یا هو یا من لا هو الا هو» is the dhikr of all things: when the veil lifts, every atom says by its fitrah «لا هو الا هو» and «لا اله الا الله» (AH P047:14-16 in one line, P048:9-11). It hands the Greatest Name to S16. Teacher Copy: al-Fatiha has no fa', the sura of subsistence and beauty (MV-S16 ¶6-9); the healing and qahr properties go to S17. ↺ B5 S04Already taught: B5 S04 "all things glorify Him, each at its own level (17:44)"New here: AH's «لا هو الا هو» said by all things by fitrah (U8.8.28); NEW: U8.8.31 (TC)

Next lesson picks up

اسمِ اعظم کیا ہے، کیا وہ چند حروف کا لفظ ہے یا کچھ اور، اور اسے کون جانتا ہے؟ (What is the Greatest Name: a word of letters or something more, and who knows it?) (S16)

S16 اسمِ اعظم اللہ کا پیدا کیا ہوا، حروف سے بنا ایک نام ہے جس کا ایک حرف بھی زمین لپیٹ دیتا ہے؛ اس کے بہتّر حرف نبی اور ائمہ کے پاس ہیں اور ایک اللہ نے اپنے پاس رکھا، آج ہم اسے نہیں جانتے مگر اسی کے واسطے سے مانگتے ہیں

ledger · 20 new · 2 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. What is the Greatest Name? A name God created «بالحروف», unlike every created thing, made one complete word of four parts; three shown for creatures' need («فالظاهر هو الله تبارك وتعالى»), one kept hidden, the «المكنون المخزون»; the hadith told once, not four times (texts 1+2; MM H1 P001:4, AH P002:3-6, JS P004 to P006 in brief, EZ P007:8-9 for "created"; U8.9.1; U8.9.13 twelve pillars and thirty names TC only; U8.9.15 AH on the Shaykhis TC only, ASK). new
  2. Is it words, or something else? Of the genus of words and letters, in the Quran; we do not know the order and vowels of its letters; it has degrees; the wali's being is a name "in its own place" but the murshid is not the Greatest Name (AH P050:2-12, P002:2, P003:12, P011:5-6 one line (home U8.8); EZ P051:5-6 for degrees; MM P049:4 «الكتاب الاسم الأكبر» ties the Name to the Book; U8.9.6, TC layer for the degrees and AH's polemic, Q6). new
  3. Who has how much of it? Seventy-three letters: the prophets were given shares and all was gathered for the Prophet; Asaf had one, the Prophet and the Imams seventy-two, one God kept «استأثر به في علم الغيب عنده» (text 7 AH P010:2, key text; text 10 JS P017:4; JS P015:4-7 on «استأثر»; EZ TAR_0053 ¶2-4 one line; U8.9.2; JS's «33» printed against the hadith's 73, Q2; MM's four-pillar report P008:3 TC only, not found in Jami, Q3). new
  4. What does one letter do, and what do seventy-two mean? Asaf spoke one letter and the earth between him and Bilqis's throne folded and returned faster than a glance; Ali: «لما عندي من الاسم الأعظم»; al-Sadiq: verses by which mountains are moved, and their knowledge is with the Imams (texts 8, 9, 11 clause only, 15 verses clause only with 17; AH P021:9-12 (ZH-S16 ¶95-97); JS P022:14-18; U8.9.3, U8.9.9; one «کن» story (AH's Jabir, Q15); the wilaya catalogue to the TC, Q9). ↺ B4 S03 · B1 S10 · B7 S12Already taught: B4 S03 "Bilqis's throne brought before she came, a miracle to prepare her for tawhid" (B1 S10 names a veil of the Greatest Name on Ali's breast; B7 S12 TC holds Asaf and the throne as power given by God)New here: Asaf did it by one letter of the Greatest Name, and the Imams, holding seventy-two, can do more
  5. How did it pass down? From prophet to prophet as a trust, the Mustahfizin who were entrusted with the Greatest Name, which is the Book, «عالماً بعد عالم» to the Prophet; then Imam to Imam: Imam Hasan gave it to Imam Husayn (texts 18+19 with 20; MM P049:3-5; EZ H2 P094:14 one line, text 44 left out; U8.9.6 MM half, U8.9.25). new
  6. Do we know it today? No: «مخزون» means hidden; the verbal Greatest Name no one now knows ("if I had it I would be Ali"); there are many names hidden in His knowledge of the unseen, not one, and the stored name is asked by in a du'a; a narrated formula is not a claim to hold it (Khidr's «يا هو يا من لا هو إلا هو» recalled); bismillah is nearer to it than the black of the eye to its white, one pointer line (home S08) (text 22; JS P055:4-7, P077:2-6, P064:3; AH P053:1 (AZ-S15 ¶20-21), P054:1-2; EZ P063:21-24, P067:5-12, AHL_0738 ¶7-9; U8.9.7, U8.9.8; U8.9.14 JS's reconciliation TC; U8.9.16, .17, .18 AH's H2 material TC or out, Q7; U8.9.26 Gabriel's tasbih TC until Ali rules, Q11; U8.8.27). [NEW, with U8.8.27 SAME: recall only]
  7. Is knowing it a safeguard, and why is it not given to everyone? Knowing the Name did not save Bal'am; a holder could misuse it in anger, as the mother who would curse her son; even its holders do not know what God has not yet decreed, the time of the Reappearance, which fits the one kept letter (EZ H2 P092:2-9, P096:16-17, P091:1-8; U8.9.23, U8.9.27 claim in the Handout and story TC, U8.9.22; U8.9.24 Satan's part TC only with 38:82-83, Q10). [NEW, with U8.9.22 PART: recall B7 S08 "none but God knows the time of the Reappearance; the Imams know the decreed, not the Hour" then new: the contrast that the Imams do know the Greatest Name, EZ AHL_0780 ¶35 (H2 P091:1-8)]
  8. What is left for us? To ask God by it as the Imams taught: «وأسألك بكل اسم هو لك ... حتى ينتهي إلى اسمك الأعظم», and by the Book «وفيه اسمك الأكبر»; AH closes his sessions so, by the great, greatest name, the Quran and the Imams; landing, and the bridge to S17 (text 34 to «اسمك الأعظم» only, the Arsh run out to Bab 5 per Q4; text 37; MM P031:2; JS P045:6; AH P033:2, P040:1 one line; EZ P048:3-7, TAR_1454 ¶28-29 one line; U8.9.4, U8.9.5, U8.11.20; RR8-010, RR8-035, RR8-041 TC). [NEW, with RR8-041 PART in the TC]

Who carries it

AH and MM carry steps 1 to 5 and 8 (MM: the Kafi hadith, the Mustahfizin, Ali by the Name, the Arafa du'a; AH: the Kafi hadith, the 73 letters, genus and degrees, hidden names, Jabir, his closing du'as). JS adds the gloss of the hadith, the prophets' shares, the verses that move mountains, the stored name; EZ adds "created", the degrees, Asaf by the Name. Step 7 is EZ alone: AH and MM are absent on the Name's limits, the unworthy who knew it and Imam Hasan's handing on (step 5 second half).

Next lesson picks up

اللہ کو اس کے ناموں سے کیسے پکارا جائے، اور کون سا نام کس حاجت کے لیے؟ (How is God called by His names, and which name for which need?) (S17)

7. Calling Him by His names S17 to S18

S17 خدا کو اس کے ناموں سے پکارا جائے: ہر حاجت میں وہ نام جو اس حاجت کے مناسب ہو، اور سکھائی ہوئی دعا انہی لفظوں میں جو سکھائے گئے

ledger · 23 new · 17 part-taught before · 3 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. What is du'a, and why by His names? Du'a is calling Him, and He set the rule Himself: «وَلِلَّهِ الْأَسْمَاءُ الْحُسْنَىٰ فَادْعُوهُ بِهَا» (7:180, brought once here), call Him by these names in every need: yā Allāh, yā Raḥmān, yā Raḥīm, yā Wadūd (JS U8.1-2 P067:1-12, P068:9-17; U8.1.17). Prayer itself is a two-way converse with the One called (AH re-read MJ-S10 ¶34-39, RR8-014, Teacher Copy). [U8.1.17 NEW; the base is U8.11.2 SAME: recall B1 S12 "du'a is mostly calling and praising Him by His names, not asking" (EZ U8.11-1 P022:2-21 sits under it); RR8-014 PART: recall B1 S16 "al-musalli munajin rabbahu" then new: munajat as mufa'ala, shared by both sides (MJ-S10 ¶34-39); RR8-062 SAME: recall only]
  2. Do we know whom we call? Du'a means calling, so a du'a must make clear who the God called is, with His names and attributes (EZ U8.11-4 P125:6-11; U8.11.42); this was the prophets' method, Joseph's «أَأَرْبَابٌ مُّتَفَرِّقُونَ خَيْرٌ أَمِ اللَّهُ الْوَاحِدُ الْقَهَّارُ» (12:39) and "read the Jawshan and see whether these attributes fit what they call god" (EZ P124:2-10; U8.11.41); asked who God is, recite the end of al-Hashr, al-Ikhlas and al-Fatiha, one 59:22-24 block for the Fasl (EZ P126:4-17; U8.11.43). [U8.11.42 PART: recall B1 S12 "Shia du'as open the road to knowing God" then new: the demand that the du'a say who is called (P125:10-11); U8.11.41 PART: recall B3 S19 "12:39 and Abraham with Nimrod for the call to tawhid" then new: the prophets state His names in the du'as (P124:6-10); U8.11.43 PART: recall B1 S12 "God introduces Himself in al-Ikhlas and 59:22" (also B7 S01 step 1) then new: EZ's rule, the three together as the answer to "who is God" (P126:7-17)]
  3. Why praise before asking, and with what praise? Ask by the attribute He has already shown: «فَأَسْأَلُ اللَّهَ الَّذِي أَكْرَمَنِي بِمَعْرِفَتِكُمْ...» (Ziyarat Ashura): before asking you name an attribute after which He will surely give (EZ U8.11-4 P120:15-18; U8.11.38). [U8.11.38 NEW; U8.11.2 SAME: recall B1 S12 and B7 S27 step 4 "praise Him by His names before the request" (JS U8.11-1 P018:1-27 with texts 1 and 2 sits under it); U8.11.8 SAME: recall B7 S23 the Rajab du'a]
  4. By which name do we ask? We ask Him by His name, and whoever calls Him by other than it gains only distance: «وَأَسْأَلُكَ بِاسْمِكَ الَّذِي مَنْ دَعَاكَ بِغَيْرِهِ لَمْ يَزْدَدْ مِنْ مَعْرِفَتِهِ بِكَ إِلَّا بُعْداً» (MM U8.11-2 P001:1, «لم يزدد» per Jami; U8.11.15); and it is beneath His greatness to send back empty the one who calls Him by His great names (AH U8.11-1 P002:2-3; U8.11.1). MM's reading of the name as the Prophet goes to a teacher note pointing to U8.10; the Greatest Name stays in S16, one bridge line. [U8.11.15 NEW; U8.11.1 PART: recall B7 S27 "praise Him by His names before asking; AH's closing du'a at the door" then new: asking by the Beautiful Names as such (MM U8.11-1 P001:1-6) and closing with Karim, 'Afuw, Ghafur, Hayy, Qayyum (AH BJ-S23 ¶134-135, Teacher Copy); U8.11.18 PART, teacher note only]
  5. Which name for which need? Each perfection asks its own manner of address: the good secretary writes one way to a king and another to a scholar, and God is all of these and the kind friend too (AH U8.11-1 P038:1-13, P038:22-44; U8.11.5); not "the Healer" at an execution but "Breaker of tyrants" (EZ P045:15-19, once); the munajat where each name grounds a plea, «أَنْتَ الْخَالِقُ وَأَنَا الْمَخْلُوقُ وَهَلْ يَرْحَمُ الْمَخْلُوقَ إِلَّا الْخَالِقُ» (JS P039:7-18); the trust of faith handed to the Amin and Hafiz (AH P049:1-14 with BJ-S22 P050; U8.11.7); Rabb, the name the du'as favour, because a perfect nurturer has every perfection, and calling Him Rabb brings neediness and nears the answer, with Zakariyya «رَبِّ إِنِّي وَهَنَ الْعَظْمُ مِنِّي» (19:3) or 3:38 in one line (JS QR_0013 ¶5-7, QR_0018 ¶6-7; EZ TOH_0040 ¶35-36, AHL_0261 ¶24-25, ¶31-32, AHL_0337 ¶29-36; U8.5.24, U8.5.25). [U8.11.5 PART: recall B1 S18 "EZ: the fitting name per occasion" and B4 S10 "Ali's Kufa munajat, Owner and owned" then new: AH's secretary (P038:10-13, 22-44) and the munajat read as name-grounded pleas (JS P039:12-18); U8.11.7 PART: recall B1 S11 "AH: each need shows a matching perfection" then new: Amin and Hafiz (AH P049:1-14); U8.5.24 NEW; U8.5.25 NEW; Teacher Copy: U8.5.36 NEW, U8.11.6 PART, U8.11.37 PART, RR8-027 NONE; RR8-033 and RR8-067 SAME: recall only]
  6. Must the words be kept as taught? For a set du'a, yes: the drowning man's prayer for the time when the Imam is not seen, «يَا اللَّهُ يَا رَحْمَانُ يَا رَحِيمُ يَا مُقَلِّبَ الْقُلُوبِ ثَبِّتْ قَلْبِي عَلَى دِينِكَ» (MM U8.11-1 P031:4-5, text 12), "say thabbit qalbi, not qulubana" (AH P032:1), and the man who added «والأبصار»: "God is Turner of hearts and eyes, but say it as I say it" (EZ P034:8-17 once, P037:26-28); each legislated name has its set property, like fifteen pumpkin seeds in a prescription: change it and the effect is lost (EZ TAR_0829 ¶51-53; U8.9.11); AH's ascetics repeating a name in forty-day retreats, one line, Teacher Copy (ZH-S19 ¶156-158; U8.9.10). [U8.11.4 NEW; U8.9.11 NEW; U8.9.10 NEW, Teacher Copy; Teacher Copy: U8.8.26 NEW, RR8-034, RR8-064, RR8-032 PART; RR8-065 SAME: recall only]
  7. What do the names do for the one who calls? The lover delights to hear the Beloved's name: Abraham gave his flocks to hear «سبوح قدوس» again (AH U8.7-2 P102:1-34, P103:1-8; U8.7.20); the name heals: «يَا مَنِ اسْمُهُ دَوَاءٌ وَذِكْرُهُ شِفَاءٌ» (Du'a Kumayl, text 10; AH ZH-S07 ¶147-153 with U8.11-1 P028:9-14, JS P030:13-14, MM Kumayl counsel one clause, verify from P027:4; U8.11.3); it shields: the Jawshan, the Prophet's armour of a thousand and one names gathered in Allah, written on the shroud (EZ P066:4-17, P067:7-14, P062:3-7 short; U8.11.11). [U8.7.20 PART: recall B4 S04 "EZ: the voice glorifying God over Abraham's flock, for Abraham's test" then new: AH's telling, love of the name «سبوح قدوس» (P102:1-34); U8.11.3 NEW; U8.11.11 PART: recall B1 S03 "the Jawshan's thousand names, read on the Nights of Qadr" then new: the armour and the shroud (P066:4-17, P067:7-14); Teacher Copy: U8.9.12, U8.9.19, U8.9.20 NEW, U8.9.21 PART, RR8-073 PART; RR8-052 SAME: recall only]
  8. Close: the Jawshan refrain «سُبْحَانَكَ يَا لَا إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا أَنْتَ الْغَوْثَ الْغَوْثَ خَلِّصْنَا مِنَ النَّارِ يَا رَبِّ» (EZ U8.11-1 P061:13-14, Jami wording with «يا»; quoted inline in B1 S12) and the khulasa; teacher note: the wayfarer's second journey, through the names, with Du'a al-Sahar (EZ U8.1-2 P073:7-18; U8.1.20). [refrain: inline earlier; U8.1.20 PART, Teacher Copy]

Who carries it

AH and MM carry steps 4 to 7: MM the asking by the name and the distance (step 4), the drowning man's prayer (step 6) and the Kumayl counsel (step 7); AH the great names that are not sent back empty (step 4), the secretary and the trust of faith (step 5), "thabbit qalbi" (step 6), Abraham's flocks and the healing name (step 7). JS carries step 1 (7:180) and the Rabb line and the munajat of step 5; EZ carries steps 2 and 3 and adds in steps 5 to 8. AH and MM are absent from steps 2 and 3 (no AH or MM record on "whom do we call" or "ask by the attribute shown"); in step 1 AH is only in the Teacher Copy (RR8-014) and the SAME recall (RR8-062).

Next lesson picks up

جب ہم اسے اس کے ناموں سے پکارتے ہیں تو کیا وہ ہر پکار کا جواب دیتا ہے، اور پھر ہر مانگ پوری کیوں نہیں ہوتی؟ (S18)

S18 وہ مجیب ہے: جو بھی اسے پکارے، گناہگار بھی، اسے ہاں کہتا ہے؛ مگر حاجت پوری کرنا ایک اور مقام ہے، اور اس کا نام دل کی توجہ اور ادب سے لیا جائے

ledger H18 · 9 new · 3 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Does He answer when called? Yes: du'a is calling one who is near («دعاه», not «ناداه»), and Mujib al-da'awat means that whoever says "O God" hears "yes"; He said so Himself (2:186); no set tongue, words, place or hour is needed, unlike the official who does not return ten greetings; pray in your own everyday tongue. (EZ H2 P074:3-28, P078:9-12, P077:1-14, P078:14-35 one image, P076:10-26, P079:2-9; AH re-read RR8-040 EJ-S12 ¶65-73; U8.11.13.) [U8.11.13 SAME against B7, kept in full: core repeat · ask, recall B7 S27 step 3 "He hears every prayer and rejects no asker" and B7 S28 step 1 "He answers every call, yes, but answering is not doing every wish", then new: the gloss of Mujib as the "yes" to a call and of «دعا» as calling the near one (EZ H2 P074:6-28) | RR8-040 NEW (B7 S07 teaches that He hears every word in any language, B7.E.76, a ground but another claim: one recall clause) | RR8-039, RR8-068, RR8-015 SAME: recall only]
  2. Then why is my request not granted after a hundred asks? Answering is one station, granting another (Mujib and Qadi al-hajat): "yes, what do you say?" is not "yes, what do you want?"; He never promised every wish, some wishes are wrong, someone else may have asked first, and nothing obliges Him to do what we say; the father says "yes, son" every time but does not hand the car key to a son with no licence; if all we wanted happened, we would overturn the world. (EZ H2 P077:1-6, P083:1-9, P082:2-11, P080:7-15, P083:11-22 one image of three (the knife and the patient's water to the Teacher Copy), P085:1-10; U8.11.23 first half; U8.11.13.) [U8.11.23 NEW (B7 S28 step 1 teaches answering is not granting with EZ's U7.43 wording; the named station Qadi al-hajat and the car key are new) | RR8-023 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B4 S17 "alms for the Imam, a human exposed to trials (AH)" then new: «الدعاء والصدقة يردان البلاء» and the rebuke of doubters (ZH-S34 ¶75) | RR8-020 SAME: recall only]
  3. What does "Hearer of the du'a" mean? Not only that He knows we spoke, which is plain, but that He hears with favour and answers; so the phrase readies the answer: the better the servant's opinion of his Lord, Generous, Rich and never stingy, the readier the answer; so ask in earnest. (JS H2 P073:2-19 with 3:38 inline; U8.11.13 second half.) [U8.11.13 new part: JS's gloss of «سميع الدعاء» as hearing with favour, not taught in B7 S27 or S28 | RR8-024, RR8-008 SAME: recall only]
  4. And Mustajib? Istijaba is the form of asking, so Mustajib al-da'awat is "I want the call answered": He wants His servants to answer Him (2:186 block, its second half «فليستجيبوا لي»; 8:24 one line) and to answer one another; the friend of God answers people quickly and is not vexed with them. (EZ H2 P100:3-14 with text 24, P101:1-31, P102:4-28 the slip in the gathering told without "I announce", P102:31-35; U8.11.23 second half.) [U8.11.23 NEW | Shimr and al-'Abbas (P101:33) ASK, Teacher Copy]
  5. May a sinner call Him too, and by which names? Yes, He calls him first: "do not despair of God's mercy" (39:53 inline) and the Friday-night call, "sinners, come; the needy, come", with the du'a «يا دائم الفضل على البرية»; he comes with no list of sins but with the penitent's munajat (text 25, cut to the first line; its close, text 27), «يا كريم، يا سريع الرضا», a tawhid of the Imam's munajat no mystic's word holds; he calls the Coverer of faults and the Knower of the unseen (text 18); and he places each lack under a name: wrong under pardon, sin under forgiveness, poverty under wealth, the perishing face under the enduring Face (text 35). ASK section: this is U8.11 lesson C folded in. (EZ H2 P074:2; JS H2 P103:1-6, P104:1-7; JS,EZ H2 P086:1-2 text 25, P086:14, EZ P087:4-12 without the names, P088:58 text 27; JS H1 P051:4 text 18; JS H3 P112:5-15 text 35; U8.11.24, U8.11.14, U8.11.32; U8.11.25 Teacher Copy.) [U8.11.24 NEW | U8.11.14 PART: recall B1 S12 "the Sahifa's munajat hold the subtleties of tawhid (JS, EZ); Ali, not Ibn Arabi, is the qibla of the gnostics (EZ)" then new: «يا كريم يا سريع الرضا» and the munajat as a tawhid no Rumi or Ibn Arabi has, in EZ H2 P087:7-12 and JS P086:14 | U8.11.32 PART: recall B7 S12 "all on earth perishes and His Face remains (55:26-27)" then new: the Mi'raj du'a placing «وجهي الفاني» under «وجهك الدائم الباقي» as a call by the names, in JS H3 P112:5-12 | U8.11.25 NEW, Teacher Copy | RR8-025, RR8-026 SAME: recall only | within the Bab: «إلهي ما أنا بأول من عصاك» is printed in S13 (U8.7.97), one recall line here]
  6. Is it the words or the heart? The heart: one dhikr with knowledge, then sleep, is better than a whole Ramadan of night vigil, though reciting without the meaning is still good, to a limit (EZ reconciles both); words pointed at a shrine with the heart elsewhere are not enough; and the name shakes only the heart that is awake: 8:2, and 59:21 with the students' scene once. (EZ H4 P123:9-26; EZ H1 P070:3-17 texts 22, 23; U8.11.40, U8.11.12.) [U8.11.40 NEW | U8.11.12 NEW | RR8-019, RR8-051, RR8-050, RR8-084 SAME: recall only | RR8-013 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B1 S02 "an hour of reflection outweighs seventy years of worship" then new: a student's night over an elementary book outweighs seventy nights of «يا قدوس», do not be fooled by beard and rosary (MJ-S02 ¶144)]
  7. What honour does His name ask? Do not spend it cheaply (AH's dervish struck for lighting a lamp with a holy name, short); adjuring by it binds (AH's Khidr sold himself as a slave when asked «بوجه الله», short); the names are written between Paradise and the Fire and hold it back (text 31, one clause); at the mention of His name, durood: Imam al-Rida on 87:15 (text 40); and the paper that carries His name is not thrown away (Teacher Copy). (AH H2 P097:1, P097:11-15 without the hall lines P097:2-10; AH H2 P098:1-9, P099 cut; MM H2 P090:3-4; JS H3 P115:24-46, P116:35-39 with 87:14-15 inline; JS H3 P117:3-15 without the late wife; U8.11.21, .22, .17, .35, .36.) [U8.11.21 NEW | U8.11.22 NEW | U8.11.17 NEW | U8.11.35 NEW | U8.11.36 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B1 S12 "the Prophet added «جل جلاله» when Zaynab al-'Attara named God, so His name is taken with reverence" then new: JS's story of the newspaper carrying God's name, in JS H3 P117:3-15]
  8. Close: who answers the one in straits? «أَمَّن يُجِيبُ الْمُضْطَرَّ إِذَا دَعَاهُ وَيَكْشِفُ السُّوءَ» (27:62); the lesson ends on the ayah, and AH's practice of having the gathering recite it aloud is a teacher note only, by recall. (AH H2 P096:1 for the ayah; its passage, the forty «يا الله», is homed in S16: if S16 prints 27:62, one recall line here.) [27:62 printed again, flagged | RR8-007 PART, Teacher Copy: recall B7 S27 step 3 "the gathering recites 27:62 aloud for their needs (AH)" then new: hands raised to God through the hands of the Imam of the Age and ten «يا الله» (AZ-S30 ¶236-242) | RR8-009, RR8-077, RR8-030, RR8-002, RR8-012, RR8-005, RR8-018, RR8-021, RR8-022 SAME: recall only]

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. 2:186 «وَإِذَا سَأَلَكَ عِبَادِي عَنِّي فَإِنِّي قَرِيبٌ ...» (text 24, EZ H2 P100:13-14; once, block; already printed in full in B1 S18).
  2. 3:38 «إِنَّكَ سَمِيعُ الدُّعَاءِ» (JS H2 P073, inline) and 8:24 (EZ H2 P102:31, one line).
  3. Munajat al-Ta'ibin «... الْخَطَايَا ثَوْبَ مَذَلَّتِي وَجَلَّلَنِي التَّبَاعُدُ مِنْكَ لِبَاسَ مَسْكَنَتِي ...» (text 25, H2 P086:1-2): Jami FOUND, Bihar 91:142, Zad al-Ma'ad 1:406-407; the spoken «الهی فبعزتك لا أجد» is not in Jami, which has «فوعزتك ما أجد لذنوبي سواك غافرا»; close «وَتَقَبَّلْ تَوْبَتِي وَكَفِّرْ خَطِيئَتِي بِمَنِّكَ وَرَحْمَتِكَ» (text 27, Bihar 91:143, FOUND). «يا سريع الرضا» (P086:14): FOUND in Du'a Kumayl (Iqbal 3; Misbah al-za'ir 1:323), not the munajat; old verified=no wrong.
  4. «اللَّهُمَّ إِنَّ ظُلْمِي أَصْبَحَ مُسْتَجِيراً بِعَفْوِكَ ...» (text 35, H3 P112:5): FOUND, Mustadrak al-Wasa'il 5:381; Jami has «ووجهي الفاني البالي», the excerpt «البالي» only. The Mi'raj gift line (P112:7-10): FOUND-OTHER-WORDING, Jami «لا حول ولا قوة إلا بالله، ولا منجى منك إلا إليك» (Mustadrak Safina 2:471); the print adds «العلي العظيم» and «لا ملجأ»: the old "no Jami source" note is wrong.
  5. «فَقَالَ كُلَّمَا ذَكَرَ اسْمَ رَبِّهِ صَلَّى عَلَى مُحَمَّدٍ وَآلِهِ» (text 40, Imam al-Rida on 87:15, H3 P116:35): FOUND, al-Kafi 2:463; extend with «وآله» from al-Kafi; «کل ما» to «كلما».
  6. «... سَبْعَةَ عَشَرَ اسْماً ... مكتوباً بين الجنة والنار» (text 31, MM H2 P090:3-4, one clause): FOUND only in Bihar 8:315 from al-Ikhtisas (disputed authorship).
  7. «يا دائم الفضل على البرية يا باسط اليدين بالعطية ...» (the Friday-night du'a, JS H2 P103, Urdu only in the print): FOUND, al-Misbah of al-Kaf'ami 1:647 (each Eid and Friday night); the writer note "not found" is wrong. 27:62 closes the lesson (already printed in full in B2 S10, B3 S11).
Next lesson picks up

S18 is the last lesson of Bab 8; it hands on to Bab 9 (tajalli), how the One we call by these names shows Himself, written when Bab 9 is planned.

How this draft was made

What this draft does (the rules applied)
  1. SAME (already taught in Bab 1 to 7): not taught again; one recall line naming the earliest lesson; the scholar's repeat sits under it in the Teacher Copy as a flagged ask.
  2. PART: the shared base recalled in one line, then the new part taught in full in the scholar's own new wording.
  3. Core repeat: kept in full and flagged (core repeat · ask).
  4. Arabic printed in full in an earlier Bab: printed again, flagged with that lesson.
  5. Imports: thin lessons only, never from an earlier Bab, kept only with a sound framing marked as Claude's judgement.
  6. Standing: Claude keeps nothing out (every cut, move or doubt is an Ask; held points stay in the Teacher Copy, flagged); AH and MM carry, EZ and JS add or confirm; Teacher Copy only; no cross-reference to other courses.
